## 2026-07-17 — ENG-P0-001 — Repository, Tooling, Documentation Migration and Test-Framework Scaffold

- **Date:** 2026-07-17
- **Phase:** TRD22 §22.10 — Phase 0, Repository and Delivery Foundation
- **Task:** ENG-P0-001 (first work package of the engineering implementation programme)
- **Status:** Implemented — awaiting Technical Review (not marked complete by the implementing agent)
- **Files changed:** see the Implementation Report for this work package (full created/modified/removed lists)
- **Tests:** Vitest unit/component tests (`apps/web`, `functions`), Playwright e2e smoke test, Firebase Emulator Suite startup/shutdown smoke validation — results recorded in the Implementation Report
- **Configuration:** pnpm workspace (`apps/web`, `functions`); root ESLint flat config, Prettier, TypeScript strict mode across every workspace; Firebase Emulator Suite configured for the `demo-11thonus` fake project (no live project, no `.firebaserc`); Husky pre-commit hook running `lint-staged`/Prettier
- **Migrations:** none (no product-domain code or Firestore schema exists yet)
- **Risks:** see the Implementation Report's Risks and Unresolved Issues section
- **Rollback:** repository has zero prior commits, so nothing needs to be reverted. Do not use `git clean -fdx` — it deletes every ignored file, including the retired-but-retained `.env.local` at the repo root. Instead, remove only the paths ENG-P0-001 introduced or intentionally replaced: `apps/ functions/ tests/ docs/ node_modules/ package.json pnpm-workspace.yaml pnpm-lock.yaml eslint.config.js .prettierrc .prettierignore .husky/ playwright.config.ts README.md firebase.json firestore.rules firestore.indexes.json storage.rules .gitignore` (see the Implementation Report §17 for the exact command). `.git/` and `.env.local` are never touched.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-implementation-report-2026-07-17.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-implementation-report-2026-07-17.md) — exists in the working tree as of this entry; not yet committed to the repository (no commit has been made at all, per the repository's zero-commit state).

- **Task:** ENG-P0-001 Closure Tracking and ENG-P0-002 Readiness Preparation (governance/tracking task — no application code, test code, or dependency changed)
- **Status:** ENG-P0-001 workflow status moved **Approved → Committed → Pushed → Complete**. Evidence for each stage:
  - **Technical Review:** Approved, both itemized corrections closed within the review (no open corrections) — [Technical Review report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-technical-review-2026-07-17.md).
  - **Commit:** `3a50710`, message `chore: establish 11thONUS phase 0 engineering foundation`, 205 files changed / 75819 insertions.
  - **Push:** `origin/main` confirmed at the same commit (`git branch -vv` → `main 3a50710 [origin/main]`); working tree clean.
  - **Definition of Done (work-package level, §2):** evaluated criterion-by-criterion against the actual repository and governing documents (not assumed):
    1. Acceptance criteria met verbatim — ✅, re-verified at Technical Review.
    2. Required tests passed — ✅.
    3. Local Validation actually run — ✅, run independently twice (implementation + review).
    4. Implementation Report produced in full — ✅.
    5. Persistent changes-tracking file updated — ✅ (this file).
    6. Technical Review Approved, no open corrections — ✅.
    7. Committed and pushed per Git Workflow — ✅ (see commit evidence above). *Minor process note, not blocking:* the commit message omits the `[<requirement/decision IDs>]` suffix specified in Git Workflow §4; the commit is already pushed and shared history is never rewritten to fix this retroactively — flagged for the next commit's habit, not corrected here.
    8. Founder deployed the change — **N/A**, not skipped: the [Engineering Implementation Programme](../05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md)'s Phase 0 profile states explicitly *"Expected Deployment State: none — Phase 0 has no deployment target"* and ENG-P0-001's own work-package row states *"Deployment Required: No."*
    9. Preview Review passed — **N/A** for the same reason (§2.8) — Preview Review presupposes a deployed environment, which does not exist at Phase 0 (Phase 1's Firebase project initialization, ENG-P1-001, remains `Blocked`).
    10. Manual Testing Standard's checklist passed — **N/A** — the Programme's Phase 0 profile states *"Manual QA Requirement: No"* and ENG-P0-001's own row states *"Manual QA Required: No"*; the Manual Testing Standard itself (§2) scopes its checklist to "every change that reaches Preview Review," which criterion 9 establishes did not occur here.
    11. No unrelated files modified — ✅, confirmed at Technical Review.
    12. Risk/rollback note remains accurate — ✅ **as of this entry** (see the corrected rollback note below; the original Implementation Report's rollback note, written when the repository had zero commits, is now superseded by the git-aware note below rather than left inaccurate).
  - **Conclusion: all applicable Definition of Done criteria are satisfied. ENG-P0-001 is marked `Complete`.**
- **Files changed:** `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md` (ENG-P0-001 → Complete with evidence; ENG-P0-002 → Ready; distribution counts updated), `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md` (ENG-P0-001 work-package row → Complete; ENG-P0-002 → Ready, blocking reason cleared; Phase 0 status → In Progress in both the profile and the Phase Summary Table), `docs/README.md` (banner status line updated to match), `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/prompts/ENG-P0-002.md` (finalized, non-draft prompt — not yet issued), `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-closure-and-eng-p0-002-readiness-report-2026-07-17.md`.
- **Tests:** none re-run in this task (no application/tooling file was touched); validated instead via `git diff --check`, `git status --short`, `git diff --stat`, `git diff --name-only`, and a full documentation relative-link check — see the closure report for exact results.
- **Migrations:** none.
- **ENG-P0-002 readiness:** moved **Blocked → Ready**. Its only blocking reason (sequential precondition "ENG-P0-001 complete") is now satisfied; its own decision dependency (DEC-TECH-004) was already CONFIRMED. A finalized, execution-ready prompt exists at [`docs/05-implementation/prompts/ENG-P0-002.md`](../05-implementation/prompts/ENG-P0-002.md), grounded in the actual repository ENG-P0-001 produced (not the original planning assumptions). **Issuing that prompt to a coding agent remains a distinct, not-yet-taken Founder/ChatGPT Technical Lead action** — this task does not issue it or begin implementing it.
- **Phase 0 status:** remains **In Progress** — ENG-P0-001 is Complete, but Phase 0 as a whole (TRD22 §22.10) is not complete until ENG-P0-002 also reaches Complete.
- **Risks:** unchanged substantively from the Implementation Report and Technical Review; the one new, minor item is the commit-message convention note under criterion 7 above (non-blocking, already pushed, not retroactively fixed).
- **Rollback (supersedes the note in the first ENG-P0-001 entry above, which was written before any commit existed and is now historically-accurate-as-written but outdated for the current state):** the repository now has one commit (`3a50710`), pushed to the shared `origin/main`. Per [Git Workflow](../06-engineering-governance/git-workflow.md) ("never force-pushes; never rewrites shared history"), rollback of a pushed commit is a **revert, not a reset**:
  ```bash
  git revert 3a50710
  git push origin main
  ```
  This creates a new commit that undoes ENG-P0-001's changes while preserving history — it does not touch `.git/` structure, `.env.local`, or force-rewrite `origin/main`. A local-only, pre-push rollback (not applicable now, since the commit is already pushed) would instead have been `git reset --hard` to the parent of `3a50710`; that option no longer applies once a commit is shared with the remote.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-closure-and-eng-p0-002-readiness-report-2026-07-17.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-001-closure-and-eng-p0-002-readiness-report-2026-07-17.md) — exists in the working tree; not yet committed (per this task's explicit instruction not to commit or push).

## 2026-07-18 — Jurisdiction and Language Baseline Correction

- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Phase:** TRD22 §22.10/§22.11 — Phase 0 closure (complete) and Phase 1 decision preparation (not started)
- **Task:** Correct the Phase 0 closure package's jurisdiction framing before Phase 1 decision work begins, per an authoritative Founder clarification (governance task — no application, test, CI, or dependency file changed)
- **Status:** Correction applied. Does not reopen or invalidate Phase 0 (still `Complete`); does not resolve DEC-LEGAL-006 or DEC-TECH-005 (both remain open); does not select a Firebase/GCP region.
- **Note on this file's own preceding entry:** the immediately preceding entry ("ENG-P0-002 — Closure and Phase 0 Completion") states its own files were "not yet committed" — that was accurate when written, but the entire Phase 0 closure package (including that entry) was committed and pushed as `c8f0bb6` on `main` before this correction task began. That statement is now superseded by this entry, per this file's own append-only rule (earlier entries are never rewritten, only superseded by a later one).
- **Founder clarification carried forward:** 11thONUS is operated and managed from **Kigali, Rwanda** (the operating jurisdiction); **Burundi is the pilot and first launch market**, not the operator's base; future East African expansion is a later concern; **supported product languages remain English and French only** (Swahili, Kinyarwanda, Kirundi not currently in scope; unchanged from the existing `CONFIRMED` decision **DEC-L10N-001**).
- **Files changed:** `docs/00-governance/decisions/dec-tech-005-firebase-region-decision-brief.md` (jurisdiction framing corrected — Rwanda-operator/Burundi-pilot-market distinction added throughout; verbatim Decision Register quotes left unaltered, with an added reading note explaining they predate this clarification; "ENG-P1-000-style task" phrase replaced with "a separately authorized engineering decision-preparation task"); `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-002-closure-and-phase-0-completion-report-2026-07-18.md` (§8 given the same jurisdiction clarification; §11 given an addendum documenting this correction; §20 updated to reference the new evidence-pack prompt) — both edited in place as ordinary governance-document corrections (not the append-only changes log, which instead receives this new entry).
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/prompts/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-pack.md` — a decision-*research* prompt (not an implementation prompt) for a future agent to gather legal (DEC-LEGAL-006) and technical (DEC-TECH-005) evidence under the corrected Rwanda-operator/Burundi-pilot-market framing and the English/French-only language constraint. **Not issued. Does not resolve either decision.**
- **Tests:** none — no application file was touched. Validated via `git diff --check`, `git status --short`, `git diff --stat`, `git diff --name-only`, and a full documentation relative-link check.
- **Configuration:** none.
- **Migrations:** none.
- **Language-governance result:** an authoritative, `CONFIRMED` decision already exists — **DEC-L10N-001** ("Launch languages EN + FR; three languages architecture-ready") — and its content already matches the Founder's restated direction exactly. No new language decision brief was created; DEC-L10N-001 was **not** modified (modifying a CONFIRMED Decision Register entry outside the formal Decision Update Procedure is out of scope, and was unnecessary since no inconsistency was found).
- **Tracking-document review result:** `coding-agent-prompt-register.md`, `engineering-implementation-programme.md`, and `docs/README.md` were reviewed for Burundi-as-home-jurisdiction, Burundi-only-hosting, or unsupported-language claims. **None found** — existing Burundi references in these documents correctly describe Burundi as the pilot/launch market (e.g. "Burundi Pilot," "Burundi electronic billing requirements"), consistent with the Founder's clarification. **None of the three was edited.**
- **Risks:** none new. See the Closure Report §11a for full detail.
- **Rollback:** this correction was applied as edits to two already-committed files (`dec-tech-005-firebase-region-decision-brief.md`, the closure report) plus one new file (the evidence-pack prompt) plus this changes-log entry — none of it committed yet at the time of this entry. Discard: `git checkout -- docs/00-governance/decisions/dec-tech-005-firebase-region-decision-brief.md docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-002-closure-and-phase-0-completion-report-2026-07-18.md docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md && rm docs/05-implementation/prompts/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-pack.md`. This does not touch `c8f0bb6` or the Phase 0 merge (`e316565`) — both remain untouched, already-shared history.
- **Report link:** this entry itself is the primary record of this correction; see also the addendum in [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-002-closure-and-phase-0-completion-report-2026-07-18.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P0-002-closure-and-phase-0-completion-report-2026-07-18.md) §11a.
- **Follow-up correction (same day, applied to this still-uncommitted entry's own files before their first commit):** two narrow fixes applied on top of the above. (1) The closure report's chronology was corrected — it previously implied this jurisdiction correction was applied *before* the original closure package's first commit; it now correctly states the original closure package was committed and pushed as `c8f0bb6`, and this jurisdiction correction is a *subsequent* governance correction applied on top of it, pending its own review and commit. (2) The evidence-pack prompt (`DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-pack.md`) was revised so its legal-research instructions (Part A, Part C, Acceptance Criteria) require the future agent to actually investigate whether Rwandan or Burundian law mandates a specific language for regulated customer communications (notices, consent, contracts, privacy disclosures) — the prompt previously pre-answered this as "English/French only," which risked suppressing a real compliance finding. Any such finding is now required to be reported as a labeled legal-compliance constraint for Founder/counsel review, explicitly distinct from and not authorizing any change to the approved product-interface language scope (still English/French only, per `DEC-L10N-001`, unchanged).

- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Phase:** Phase 1 decision preparation (Phase 1 itself remains `Blocked`; this is evidence-gathering, not implementation)
- **Task:** Execution of the [DEC-LEGAL-006 / DEC-TECH-005 evidence-gathering prompt](../05-implementation/prompts/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-pack.md), committed at `fcd89d4` — real internet research producing citation-backed evidence packs for Founder and legal-counsel review
- **Status:** **This is decision evidence only.** Neither DEC-LEGAL-006 nor DEC-TECH-005 is resolved by this work; no Firebase/GCP region has been selected; Phase 1 is not Ready; no Firebase project was created; nothing was deployed. Both decisions remain exactly as open as before this research — `OPEN_LEGAL` and `OPEN_ENGINEERING` respectively — in the (untouched) Decision Register.
- **Files changed:** none — this task only created new files.
- **Files created:** [Legal Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-cross-border-hosting-evidence-2026-07-18.md); [Technical Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-TECH-005-firebase-region-evaluation-2026-07-18.md); [Proposed (Unapplied) Decision Updates](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-proposed-updates-2026-07-18.md); [Founder Brief](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-founder-brief-2026-07-18.md); [Source Register](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-source-register-2026-07-18.md); [Evidence-Gathering Report](../05-implementation/reports/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-gathering-report-2026-07-18.md).
- **Tests:** documentation relative-link check only (no application file touched — see the Evidence-Gathering Report for the exact result).
- **Configuration:** none.
- **Migrations:** none.
- **Key legal finding:** Rwanda's Law N° 058/2021 requires data storage inside Rwanda by default (Article 50), with NCSA authorization or a documented alternative ground (Article 48) plus a written transfer contract (Article 49) required for any offshore hosting — this applies to any Firebase/GCP region, since none is located in Rwanda. Burundi adopted its first comprehensive data protection law only in March 2026 (Loi n° 1/03 du 10 mars 2026); its specific cross-border transfer provisions could not be confirmed from public sources in this research pass (the primary text could not be directly retrieved) and are the single largest open item before DEC-LEGAL-006 can be resolved.
- **Key technical finding:** `africa-south1` (Johannesburg) is Google's only African region and the likely lowest-latency candidate for Kigali/Bujumbura (estimated, not measured), but does not support Cloud Scheduler; `europe-west1` (Belgium) offers full confirmed service coverage with no such gap. Neither is recommended as final — both require legal confirmation and an actual latency measurement first.
- **Risks:** see the Evidence-Gathering Report §16 — primarily the Burundi-law confirmation gap and the reliance on latency/pricing estimates rather than measurements.
- **Rollback:** all six files created by this task are new and untracked; discard by deleting them (exact list in the Evidence-Gathering Report §17). Nothing else was touched.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-gathering-report-2026-07-18.md`](../05-implementation/reports/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-gathering-report-2026-07-18.md) — exists in the working tree; not yet committed (per this task's explicit instruction not to commit or push).

- **Files changed (all five evidence-package files from the preceding entry, edited in place, plus this changes log and the report itself):**
  - [Legal Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-cross-border-hosting-evidence-2026-07-18.md) — Rwanda's official-language count corrected (four: Kinyarwanda, English, French, Kiswahili, not three); §3 Burundi section rewritten with confirmed primary-text findings (territorial scope, definitions, lawful bases, cross-border transfer mechanism, data subject rights, major-controller threshold, the data protection authority's name, security obligations, breach notification, sanctions, transitional deadlines); §5, §6, §7, §8 updated to match.
  - [Technical Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-TECH-005-firebase-region-evaluation-2026-07-18.md) — service matrix (§2) completed for Cloud Storage, Cloud Run, Cloud Tasks (a new confirmed gap for `africa-south1`), App Check, Pub/Sub, Secret Manager, and Firestore backup/export; Firebase Authentication wording corrected to match what the documentation actually states; recommendation language (§7, §9) softened to avoid overstating "full" service coverage.
  - [Proposed Decision Updates](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-proposed-updates-2026-07-18.md) — updated to reflect the confirmed Burundi mechanism and the Cloud Tasks gap; both proposed statuses remain `OPEN_LEGAL`/`OPEN_ENGINEERING`, unchanged.

  - [Evidence-Gathering Report](../05-implementation/reports/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-gathering-report-2026-07-18.md) — updated throughout to match the corrected findings and source counts.

- **What changed and why:** the original evidence pack (immediately preceding entry) understated Rwanda's official-language count and could not retrieve Burundi's primary statutory text (a network/certificate error blocked the fetch), so it reported Burundi's cross-border mechanism, registration thresholds, and breach-notification rules as "unconfirmed." This correction pass **successfully downloaded Burundi's law directly** (a 32-page, image-only PDF with no text layer; no OCR tool was available, so all 55 articles were reviewed visually from rendered page images) and found it to use an **adequacy-based cross-border transfer mechanism** (Articles 15–16), not a Rwanda-style localization default — closing the largest gap in the original pass, though two narrower Burundi-specific gaps remain (the Ministerial adequacy list's contents; the new Agency's operational status). The technical service matrix was also completed — most notably, **Cloud Tasks, not just Cloud Scheduler, is confirmed unsupported in `africa-south1`**, a materially larger technical caveat for that candidate than the original pass identified.
- **Risks:** the Burundi law review was manual (visual page-by-page reading, not OCR or machine-verified) — a second reviewer or OCR-based cross-check remains prudent before final legal reliance, even though all 55 articles were covered. See the Evidence-Gathering Report §16 for the full, corrected risk list.

- **Phase:** Phase 1 decision preparation (Phase 1 itself remains `Blocked`; this is a further narrow correction to evidence-gathering, not implementation)
- **Task:** Final narrow consistency correction of the DEC-LEGAL-006/DEC-TECH-005 evidence package — a follow-up to the two immediately preceding entries, addressing three claims that remained stronger than their supporting evidence even after the first correction pass
- **Status:** **Correction applied. This is still decision evidence only.** Neither DEC-LEGAL-006 nor DEC-TECH-005 is resolved; no Firebase/GCP region has been selected; Phase 1 is not Ready; no Firebase project was created; nothing was deployed; the Decision Register was not modified (both decisions remain `OPEN_LEGAL` / `OPEN_ENGINEERING`).
- **Files changed (all five evidence-package files, edited in place, plus this changes log and the report itself):**
  - **Firebase Authentication residency wording narrowed** across the [Legal Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-cross-border-hosting-evidence-2026-07-18.md), [Technical Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-TECH-005-firebase-region-evaluation-2026-07-18.md), [Proposed Decision Updates](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-proposed-updates-2026-07-18.md), [Founder Brief](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-founder-brief-2026-07-18.md), [Source Register](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-source-register-2026-07-18.md) (T6, S11), and the [Evidence-Gathering Report](../05-implementation/reports/DEC-LEGAL-006-DEC-TECH-005-evidence-gathering-report-2026-07-18.md) — the controlled statement is now "Firebase Authentication does not expose a project-level location-selection control comparable to Firestore or Cloud Storage," with an explicit added limitation that the officially reviewed documentation does not establish Authentication's precise data residency, and that confirmation must come from Google's service terms, data-processing documentation, or the provider directly. The Proposed Decision Update now requests provider confirmation rather than asserting a residency fact.

Cloud Scheduler retains its documented/strongly-supported cross-region HTTP-target pattern. Cloud Tasks is now explicitly stated as an unresolved architecture question for `africa-south1`, with its own list of open sub-questions (cross-region invocation security, authentication method, retry/dead-letter behavior, regional-failure behavior, latency/egress, and data-residency implications for task payloads) added to the Technical Evidence Pack §9 and the Evidence-Gathering Report.

The confirmed comparison (europe-west1 has the more complete confirmed service profile; africa-south1 requires additional architecture decisions) is preserved, and the confirmed Scheduler/Tasks findings themselves were not downgraded.
  - **Founder Brief §4 rewritten** to explain, in plain language: Belgium offers the simpler single-region path among checked candidates; Johannesburg's latency advantage is estimated, not measured; Johannesburg lacks both Scheduler and Tasks; Scheduler has a workable cross-region design; Tasks needs a separate answer; and Firebase Authentication's exact data-location position requires provider confirmation. - **Files created:** [DEC-LEGAL-006 Cross-Border Hosting and Data Residency Evidence Pack](../00-governance/decisions/DEC-LEGAL-006-Cross-Border-Hosting-and-Data-Residency-Evidence-Pack.md) (§0 pre-work analysis; §1 Rwanda; §2 Burundi, with established-law/draft-law/absence-of-regulation distinguished explicitly; §3 regional frameworks — Malabo, EAC, COMESA; §4 Google Cloud DPA/SCCs/certifications, new research this pass; §5 cross-border technical implications only, no legal conclusions; §6 risks; §7 outstanding-questions checklist for counsel, unanswered by design; §8 engineering implications; §9 one-page Founder Decision Summary); [Implementation Report](../05-implementation/reports/engineering-decision-sprint-0c-dec-legal-006-evidence-report-2026-07-19.md).
- **Files changed:** none — confirmed via `git status --short` that no previously-tracked governance document was touched, per the task's explicit "Do NOT modify Decision Register / governance / hierarchy" constraints.
- **Tests:** none — no application file was touched. Validated via `git diff --check` (clean); a full documentation relative-link check (1,455 links across 174 files, 0 broken); an explicit grep confirming no `CONFIRMED`/approval language attaches to either `DEC-LEGAL-006` or `DEC-TECH-005` anywhere in the new document; a consistency check between the new pack's Engineering Implications (§8) and the existing Engineering Implementation Programme's Phase 1 blocking structure (confirmed unaltered).
- **Configuration:** none.
- **Migrations:** none.
- **Built on, not duplicative of, prior research:** a substantial 2026-07-18 legal evidence pack already existed, grounded in a full, direct, page-by-page review of Burundi's 55-article statute (a scanned PDF with no text layer, reviewed from rendered page images) and Rwanda's primary statutory text. This new pack cites and extends that research rather than re-deriving it, adding exactly what the prior pack did not cover: Google Cloud's DPA/SCC/certification framework (finding: Google's public SCCs are built around EU/UK/Swiss transfer law and do not reference Rwanda's or Burundi's own mechanisms by name — a material, newly-disclosed gap), South Africa's Malabo Convention ratification status (finding: signed, not ratified — closing an item the prior pack had left open), and the EAC's/COMESA's regional-framework applicability (finding: EAC has a developing, not-yet-adopted cross-border data-flow mechanism; no COMESA-specific instrument was found relevant).
- **Risks:** the South Africa/Malabo and EAC findings rest on search-synthesized secondary reporting rather than a directly-fetched primary AU/EAC document in every case — flagged in the pack itself as a re-verification recommendation; a discrepancy between a secondary EAC source and the already-primary-source-confirmed Burundi statute was found and disclosed rather than silently resolved.
- **Rollback:** the change is additive only (one new document plus this report) — see the Implementation Report §11 for rollback guidance.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/engineering-decision-sprint-0c-dec-legal-006-evidence-report-2026-07-19.md`](../05-implementation/reports/engineering-decision-sprint-0c-dec-legal-006-evidence-report-2026-07-19.md) — exists in the working tree; not yet committed (this task did not instruct a commit).

## 2026-07-20 — ENG-P1-001 — Firebase & Shared Platform Foundation

- **Date:** 2026-07-20
- **Phase:** First application-code implementation task of the Version 1.0 Engineering Implementation Programme — platform foundation only; no business logic, UI, authentication flow, or domain service implemented.
- **Task:** Implement ENG-P1-001: environment-aware configuration loading, Firebase client SDK initialization (App, Auth, Firestore, Storage, App Check) for `apps/web`, and Firebase Admin SDK initialization plus the approved `europe-west1` region constant (`DEC-TECH-005`) for `functions` — built test-first (TDD), validated against the real Firebase Emulator Suite.
- **Status:** **Implemented — Under Review.** All local and emulator validation passes; one self-review defect found and corrected (see below); not committed or pushed (no commit instructed for this task); Technical Review not yet performed.
- **Files created:** `apps/web/src/config/env.ts`/`env.test.ts` (environment loading, 8 tests); `apps/web/src/vite-env.d.ts` (`ImportMetaEnv` typing, previously missing); `apps/web/src/infrastructure/firebase/{app,auth,firestore,storage,appCheck}.ts` and their `.test.ts` files (18 tests total); `apps/web/src/infrastructure/firebase/index.ts`/`index.test.ts` (composition root, 3 tests); `functions/src/config/region.ts`/`region.test.ts` (`PLATFORM_REGION = "europe-west1"`, 1 test); `functions/src/infrastructure/firebase/admin.ts`/`admin.test.ts` (Admin SDK singleton, 2 tests); `.env.example` (variable names only, no values); [Implementation Report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-implementation-report-2026-07-20.md).
- **Files changed:** `apps/web/package.json` (added `firebase ^12.16.0`); `apps/web/src/main.tsx` (wired `initializeFirebasePlatform(getAppEnv())` before render); `functions/src/index.ts` (added `setGlobalOptions({ region: PLATFORM_REGION, ... })` and `getAdminApp()`); `functions/src/index.test.ts` (added a region-assertion test using the `ping` function's `__endpoint.region`); `pnpm-lock.yaml` (auto-updated by `pnpm add firebase`); [Engineering Implementation Programme](../05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md) (`ENG-P1-001` → `Under Review`); [Coding-Agent Prompt Register](../05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md) (`ENG-P1-001` → `Under Review`, §5 distribution updated).
- **Tests:** 32/32 passing (`apps/web`: 8 test files / 27 tests; `functions`: 3 test files / 5 tests) — every file built Red→Green per TDD, confirmed failing for the correct reason before each fix. Full workspace validation: `pnpm -r run typecheck` (both workspaces, strict, clean), `pnpm lint` (zero findings), `pnpm format:check` (clean, after one `pnpm format` auto-fix during validation), `pnpm -r run build` (both workspaces build), `pnpm emulators:validate` (real Firebase Emulator Suite — confirms `functions[europe-west1-ping]: http function initialized`, direct tool evidence of correct region wiring, not just a unit-test assertion).
- **Configuration:** new `.env.example` at repository root documents every `VITE_*` variable (required and optional) by name only; `.gitignore` already anticipated this file (`!.env.example`).
- **Migrations:** none.
- **Self-review defect found and corrected (TDD):** `env.ts`'s `parseBoolean` originally coerced any non-`"true"` `VITE_USE_FIREBASE_EMULATOR` value (e.g. a typo like `"yes"`) silently to `false` instead of erroring. Added a failing test, confirmed RED, corrected `parseBoolean` to throw a clear, named error for any value that isn't exactly `"true"`/`"false"`/unset, confirmed GREEN (8/8 `env.test.ts`), re-ran the full suite (32/32, no regression).
- **Explicit, disclosed scope boundary — not a shortfall:** no live `europe-west1` Firebase project was created. The Cloud Environment & Deployment Strategy §7 (Infrastructure Governance) states project creation is never a coding agent's autonomous action; it requires Founder authorization and billing-account access this agent does not have. All code was built and validated against the pre-existing Firebase Emulator Suite (`demo-11thonus`) instead, written so pointing it at a real `europe-west1` project later requires only populating `.env` values — no code change. The pre-existing `.env.local` (wrong-region project, `nam5`/`us-east1`) was not touched or used.
- **Risks:** App Check cannot be validated against a real reCAPTCHA site key until a live project exists (returns `undefined` with a clear `console.warn` by design, not a silent gap); `apps/web` build now emits a non-blocking chunk-size warning (779.69 kB / 237.28 kB gzip) after adding the `firebase` package — informational, not a failure, and out of this work package's minimal-infrastructure scope to address. See the Implementation Report §10 for the full risk list.
- **Rollback:** all changes in this task are uncommitted — see the Implementation Report §11 for the full per-file rollback list.

## 2026-07-20 — ENG-P1-001 Independent Technical Review

- **Date:** 2026-07-20
- **Phase:** Independent Technical Review of ENG-P1-001 — read-only; no implementation code, test, dependency, or Firebase configuration was modified. Performed by a distinct reviewer pass from the implementing session, treating shared authorship as a disclosed conflict of interest.
- **Task:** Independently re-verify ENG-P1-001's implementation against the live repository — every source/test file re-read in full, every validation command re-run from a clean shell, the Firebase Emulator Suite independently started, every claim in the Implementation Report checked against actual command output rather than accepted on its own authority — and render a verdict of Approved, Approved with Non-Blocking Observations, or Corrections Required.
- **Status:** **Complete.** Verdict: **Approved with non-blocking observations.** Zero Critical or blocking-High findings.
- **Files created:** [ENG-P1-001 Technical Review](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-technical-review-2026-07-20.md) (30-section report: independent validation results, architecture/configuration/App-Check/Admin/region/emulator/test-quality/dependency/security/scope-compliance review, implementation-report accuracy comparison table, live-project-provisioning governance assessment, findings table, verdict).
- **Files changed:** [Engineering Implementation Programme](../05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md) (`ENG-P1-001` row and Phase 1 status narrative: `Under Review` → `Approved`; Technical Review link added; `ENG-P1-002` left `Blocked`); [Coding-Agent Prompt Register](../05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md) (`ENG-P1-001` row: `Under Review` → `Approved`; Technical Review link added; §5 distribution updated).
- **Tests:** independently re-ran the full validation suite from a clean shell — `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm -r run typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm -r run build`, `pnpm -r run test` (verbose, every one of 32 test names individually inspected), `pnpm emulators:validate` (independently confirmed `functions[europe-west1-ping]` initializes correctly). All reproduced with identical results to the Implementation Report's claims. Additional independent checks: repository-wide region-string grep, secret-pattern grep, direct-SDK-import grep (none outside the composition layer), duplicate-initialization-path grep (none found), and a targeted empirical test of Vite's env-loading behavior (see Key Finding below).
- **Configuration:** none — read-only review.
- **Migrations:** none.
- **Findings (4, all Medium/Low, none blocking):** (1) **CFG-1 (Medium)** — a `.env.example` already existed at `apps/web/.env.example` (tracked since ENG-P0-001); ENG-P1-001 created a second, overlapping, now-inconsistent `.env.example` at the repository root instead of reconciling with it, and the Implementation Report's claim that no `.env.example` previously existed is factually inaccurate. Recommended follow-up: consolidate into one canonical file. (2) **AC-1 (Medium)** — App Check's missing-site-key handling (`appCheck.ts:25-31`) is identical in dev and any future production build (warn-and-continue in both); not reachable today (no production build target/live project exists) but should be hardened to throw in non-dev mode before a production `.env` is ever populated. (3) **AT-1 (Low)** — `app.test.ts`'s `afterEach` hook's comment overstates what the code does (claims cleanup; only toggles an unrelated property); real test isolation is via randomized app names. (4) **AD-1 (Low)** — `admin.test.ts`'s two tests are order-dependent within the file (harmless; no cross-file pollution, confirmed via Vitest's default per-file isolation).
- **Key finding — one of the Implementation Report's own disclosed risks was independently tested and found overstated:** the report's §10 warned that running `pnpm dev` without `VITE_USE_FIREBASE_EMULATOR=true` would connect to the wrong-region `.env.local` project. Independent testing (confirming `pnpm --filter web exec pwd` resolves to `apps/web/`, then using Vite's own `loadEnv()` utility from that exact working directory) found **zero** `VITE_*` variables are loaded from the repository-root `.env.local` under the real invocation path — `apps/web/vite.config.ts` sets no `envDir`, so Vite's env directory defaults to the package's own working directory, which has no `.env.local` of its own. The actual failure mode is a loud, immediate `loadEnv()` throw ("Missing required environment variables..."), not a silent wrong-region connection — safer than what was disclosed.
- **Live-project provisioning governance conclusion:** the governing documents (Cloud Environment & Deployment Strategy §7; Definition of Done §2, where item 6 "Technical Review Approved" precedes items 7–8 "commit/push"/"Founder deploy"; the Decision Register's `DEC-TECH-005`/`DEC-LEGAL-006` CONFIRMED entries) support **Option B**: the code is technically approvable now; dev/staging project provisioning is a distinct, Founder/Engineering-Lead-owned action required before `ENG-P1-001` reaches **Complete** (and therefore before Phase 1 completion), not before Technical Approval, and not deferred to a later work package. `ENG-P1-001` is **not** marked `Complete` by this review.
- **Risks:** none newly introduced by this review (read-only); the four findings above are the only residual items, each with a stated non-blocking rationale and recommended follow-up in the Technical Review report §21–22.
- **Rollback:** all changes in this task are uncommitted — see the Technical Review §30 for the full per-file rollback list.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-technical-review-2026-07-20.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-technical-review-2026-07-20.md) — exists in the working tree; not yet committed (this task did not instruct a commit).

This is a platform naming-format constraint common to all four approved candidates, not an availability/uniqueness conflict, and not fixable by retrying. Per this task's explicit instruction ("Do not invent another ID"), no substitute ID was chosen. `ENG-P1-001` remains **Approved** — not `Complete`. `ENG-P1-002` remains **Blocked** and was **not started**.
- **Files created:** [ENG-P1-001 Firebase Environment Provisioning Report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-firebase-environment-provisioning-2026-07-21.md) (26-section report per the task's required structure: pre-provisioning analysis, all four project-ID attempts with verbatim error evidence, billing-account display names, unrelated-project isolation confirmation, required Founder action, rollback).

- **Tests:** none — no application file was touched; this task never reached any code-affecting step. The full validation suite (34/34 tests) from the prior closure task remains the current, accurate, unaffected state.
- **Verified fact — Firestore location mapping resolved for future reuse:** `gcloud firestore locations list` (a read-only global-catalogue query, run against the pre-existing `eleventh-on-us` project without modifying it) confirmed `europe-west1` is directly selectable as a Firestore Native single-region `locationId`, consistent with `DEC-TECH-005`, distinct from the `eur3` multi-region. This finding remains valid for the next provisioning attempt and does not need to be re-derived.
- **Verified fact — all four project-ID attempts, with identical root cause:** `gcloud projects create 11thonus-dev`, `11thonus-dev-rw`, `11thonus-staging`, `11thonus-staging-rw` — each rejected client-side before any server-side availability check: *"Bad value: Project IDs are immutable... They must start with a lowercase letter... Project IDs must be between 6 and 30 characters."* Every approved candidate starts with the digit `1`. `firebase projects:list` confirmed zero `11thonus`-named projects exist after all four attempts.
- **Verified fact — no side effect on unrelated resources:** `eleventh-on-us` independently re-confirmed unchanged (`lifecycleState: ACTIVE`) after all four attempts; no other project on the account was referenced by any command; no `gcloud config set project` was run, so the ambient active project was never relied upon.
- **Billing (gathered ahead of schedule, since Part D was never reached in its intended post-creation sequence):** four open billing accounts exist on the account, listed by display name only (no account IDs, per the task's explicit instruction) — three share the generic, Firebase-auto-generated name "Firebase Payment," and none has a display name referencing 11thONUS. Per the task's own evidentiary bar, no account is recommended; billing attachment remains unauthorized and untouched.

- **Dependencies:** none added.
- **Required Founder action:** new project IDs that satisfy GCP's naming rule (must start with a lowercase letter) for both Development and Staging; selection of exactly one of the four open billing accounts (by the Founder's own record-keeping, since display names alone cannot distinguish the three identically-named accounts) for whenever billing becomes relevant. No other prior Founder decision (owning account, production timing) is affected — only the project IDs themselves need to change for a retry.

Two local-repository drifts found and corrected (below). **Infrastructure closure criteria satisfied** — a criteria-satisfaction statement only, not a work-package status transition. `ENG-P1-001` remains **Approved** — not `Complete`. `ENG-P1-002` remains **Blocked**, not started.

- **Files modified:** `.firebaserc` (removed an unauthorized `"default": "eleventh-on-us"` key that had appeared since the last verification — violates the standing "no default alias" rule; now contains only `dev`/`staging`, exactly as approved); `storage.rules` (restored to the deny-by-default baseline after being found overwritten with Firebase's open test-mode template — a local-file drift, not a live security exposure, see below); `firebase.json` (cosmetic-only reformat via the repository's own `pnpm format` tooling, confirmed identical to the tracked baseline afterward); [Engineering Implementation Programme](../05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md) (dated note only; `Status` field unchanged, `Approved`); [Coding-Agent Prompt Register](../05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md) (dated note and report link only; `Status` field unchanged, `Approved`).
- **Verified facts — billing:** both `eleventh-on-us-dev` and `eleventh-on-us-staging` now have billing enabled, sharing the **same** active/open billing account (Staging's Blaze upgrade confirmed successful — a change since the prior task). Account identifiers redacted per instruction.
- **Verified facts — Storage buckets:** exactly one bucket per project (`eleventh-on-us-dev.firebasestorage.app`, `eleventh-on-us-staging.firebasestorage.app`), both `location: EUROPE-WEST1` / `location_type: region`, both `REGIONAL` storage class, both zero objects, both correctly owned (ACL entities matched to each project's own project number), neither with a public ACL grant, neither with a lifecycle or retention policy, both with GCS's standard 7-day soft-delete default (a platform default, not Founder-configured). `public_access_prevention: inherited` on both — a non-blocking observation (no GCP organization exists to enforce it at the IAM layer), mitigated by the Storage Security Rules layer denying all access regardless.
- **Key finding, independently resolved — live Storage Rules vs. the local repository file:** both projects' **live** Storage Rules, queried directly via the Firebase Rules API (`firebaserules.googleapis.com`, not the local file), are the restrictive deny-by-default set (`allow read, write: if false;`) — confirming the Founder's Console rule selection succeeded on both. The **local** `storage.rules` file, however, had been separately overwritten with Firebase's default open test-mode template (a 30-day-expiring, fully-open rule) — a `git diff` against the tracked baseline confirmed this was new drift, most likely a side effect of a local `firebase init storage` run, unrelated to and not reflective of the Console-based rule selection. **The live rules were never open at any point this task could observe** — this was corrected as a local-file-only discrepancy, not a live security remediation.
- **Tests:** none new. Full repository validation re-run (required due to the `.firebaserc`/`storage.rules`/`firebase.json` corrections): `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm -r run typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` (one auto-fix for `firebase.json`, confirmed identical to baseline after), `pnpm -r run build`, `pnpm -r run test` (34/34, unchanged), `pnpm emulators:validate` — independently re-confirmed `functions[europe-west1-ping]` under `demo-11thonus`, with the Storage emulator now loading the corrected (more secure, never less secure) deny-by-default rules.
- **Read-only checks (both projects), unchanged from prior tasks:** Firestore remains `europe-west1`/`FIRESTORE_NATIVE`; zero Auth users (`CONFIGURATION_NOT_FOUND`); `cloudfunctions.googleapis.com`/`run.googleapis.com` not enabled, zero Functions/Cloud Run possible; one auto-registered Hosting site each, no release deployed; no Web App registered on either project; App Check API enabled on Development only (unchanged), no reCAPTCHA API on either, no site key anywhere.
- **Configuration:** `.firebaserc` and `storage.rules` corrected as described above. No live Firebase/GCP configuration was changed — every live-infrastructure command in this task was read-only (`describe`, `list`, `ls`, and `GET`-only Rules-API calls).
- **Dependencies:** none added.
- **Infrastructure closure assessment — all criteria satisfied:** both projects Firebase-enabled; both billing-attached and active; both Firestore in `europe-west1`; both Storage in `europe-west1`; both buckets empty; no public/open live Storage Rules; no unintended deployment; aliases correct (after correction); emulator isolation intact; no secrets tracked; all validation passes. **Infrastructure closure criteria satisfied** — the formal `Complete` transition remains a separate, not-yet-authorized task (commit, push, CI, Definition-of-Done reconciliation).
- **Risks:** `public_access_prevention: inherited` on both buckets is a non-blocking hardening observation for future work (plausibly ENG-P1-003's scope), not a defect; the exact local action that produced the `.firebaserc`/`storage.rules` drift could not be directly observed, only diagnosed from its effects.

## 2026-07-22 — ENG-P1-001 Formal Closure, Commit, Push, CI

- **Date:** 2026-07-22
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — ENG-P1-001 closure
- **Task:** Execute `ENG-P1-001-CLOSE`: audit and classify the entire dirty working tree, confirm the ENG-P1-001 change set is complete and internally consistent, run full local validation, reconcile the Definition of Done, and commit/push/verify CI through the governed Git Workflow.
- **Status:** **`ENG-P1-001` moved `Approved → Pushed`** — committed, pushed, CI passed on the exact pushed commit, PR opened. **Not** moved to `Complete`: Git Workflow does not authorize a coding agent to merge into `main` (only the Founder deploys from a protected `main`), so the Founder-controlled merge decision on the opened PR was not executed by this task. `ENG-P1-002` remains `Blocked` — its precondition is `ENG-P1-001` **complete**, which `Pushed`/`Approved for merge` does not yet satisfy.
- **Dirty-worktree audit:** the working tree mixed ENG-P1-001/Master-Workflow work with an entirely separate, unrelated Loyalty-domain governance backlog (Verified Loyalty freeze, Decision Sprint 1, and their PRD/TRD/Decision-Register/RTM edits) accumulated across this whole session and never previously committed. Every dirty path was classified; the unrelated backlog was left untouched and uncommitted for its own future closure task. Three shared tracking files (`docs/README.md`, the Engineering Implementation Programme, this change log) had ENG-P1-001-relevant edits interleaved with unrelated ones in the same file — their relevant hunks were staged directly via `git hash-object`/`git update-index`, never `git add .`/`-A`/`git commit -a`.
- **Defect found and fixed:** `pnpm test:e2e` failed — `env.ts` unconditionally threw when required Firebase config is missing (true in this environment; no Web App is registered on either live project yet), crashing the app before render. Fixed via TDD: `env.ts` now falls back to a safe, non-secret demo config in emulator mode; `playwright.config.ts`'s e2e build now explicitly runs in emulator mode. Re-verified clean in both local validation and CI.
- **Live infrastructure reconfirmed (read-only):** both `eleventh-on-us-dev`/`eleventh-on-us-staging` — active, billing enabled, Firestore `europe-west1`, exactly one empty bucket each at `EUROPE-WEST1`, live Storage Rules deny-by-default (re-queried via the Rules API), no Functions/Web Apps/application Hosting content, matching the 2026-07-21 evidence exactly.
- **Local validation:** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`, `build`, `test` (36/36), `test:e2e` (1/1, after the fix), `emulators:validate`, `git diff --check` — all pass. Doc-link validation across every staged file (checked against post-commit state) found only one disclosed, narrow exception: `version-1-engineering-baseline-declaration.md`'s own 5 links into the excluded Loyalty-domain files.
- **Definition of Done:** reconciled against all 12 criteria in `docs/06-engineering-governance/definition-of-done.md` §2 — see the [Closure Report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-closure-report-2026-07-22.md) §7 for the full per-criterion table. Criteria 1–6, 11–12 satisfied; criterion 7 satisfied by this task; criteria 8–9 (Founder pull/deploy/Preview Review) remain pending Founder action; criterion 10 (Manual QA) not applicable at this stage per the Programme's own Phase 1 profile.
- **Files created:** [ENG-P1-001 Closure Report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-001-closure-report-2026-07-22.md).
- **Files changed:** `apps/web/src/config/env.ts`/`env.test.ts` (demo-config fallback fix), `playwright.config.ts` (emulator-mode e2e build), plus the Master Workflow (§7/§8/§17), Engineering Implementation Programme, and Coding-Agent Prompt Register (all updated to `Pushed`/`Approved for merge`).
- **Git strategy:** feature branch `chore/eng-p1-001-closure` (Git Workflow §7 — large, long-running work package; matches `ENG-P0-002`'s own precedent).
- **Commit:** `ba43da1` on `chore/eng-p1-001-closure`.
- **Push:** `origin/chore/eng-p1-001-closure`.
- **Pull Request:** [#2](https://github.com/Fkenogo/11THONUS/pull/2).
- **CI:** [run 29916547244](https://github.com/Fkenogo/11THONUS/actions/runs/29916547244) — **passed**, verified against `headSha` `ba43da147cd0d179284c9ac271af32848c141d76` (the exact pushed commit).

## 2026-07-25 — ENG-P1-002: Shared Engineering Foundation Implementation

- **Date:** 2026-07-25
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11).
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-002` — implement the shared `authenticate → validate → log → respond` command foundation every later domain service depends on, per the approved [Engineering Blueprint](../05-implementation/prompts/ENG-P1-002-engineering-blueprint-2026-07-25.md), realizing (not redesigning) the engineering contracts TRD11/TRD10/TRD20 already approve.
- **Entry verification:** `origin/main` at `d1c458b356abdb484f4df517fead5a5e0f24d45f`; `ENG-P1-002` confirmed `Ready`; `DEC-TECH-006`/`DEC-TECH-007` confirmed `CONFIRMED`; the EIR governance stream confirmed at `EIR-03`. The Engineering Blueprint's own PR (#11) was still open at the time this task began — not treated as a stop condition, since the Founder's own task message is itself the specific, written authorization.
- **Implemented:** Command Contract (`CommandEnvelope<T>`), Event Contract (`DomainEvent<T>` + naming standard), correlation-ID service, Logging Contract (`OperationalLog` + shared logger with a sensitive-content guard), Idempotency service (Firestore-backed `idempotencyRecords` collection), Event Outbox (schema + transactional writer + retry/dead-letter processor with bounded exponential backoff), shared Error Contract (`PlatformErrorResponse` + TRD11 §11.35's 14 categories), shared actor/request validation, and `dispatchCommand` tying all of the above into the one reusable command shape. Every type is copied field-for-field from already-approved TRD text — no new architecture introduced.
- **Test-first (TDD) throughout:** every module written test-first, RED confirmed for the correct reason, then GREEN. 87/87 unit tests pass (`pnpm test`, no emulator needed); 14/14 integration tests pass against a real Firebase Emulator Suite Firestore instance (`pnpm emulators:validate`, a new `test:emulator` script — replacing a prior placeholder smoke check with genuine coverage).
- **Two real defects found and fixed** via this same process: the logger's sensitive-content guard initially false-positived on this project's own `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE` error-category values and `lower_snake_case` dispatcher result labels (both are long, letters-plus-underscore strings colliding with a "long token" heuristic) — caught by the real emulator run, fixed via a failing regression test then a corrected regex, confirmed by a clean subsequent full run.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no Firestore Security Rules authored; no new deployed Cloud Function added; no domain command, event, or business rule written; no application code outside `functions/src/shared/` touched; no governance document expanded — only existing tracker status fields and the standing changes logs were updated. `ENG-P1-002` status moved `Ready` → `Under Review` (Technical Review still required before `Complete`).
- **Files created:** 18 source modules + 20 test files (17 unit, 3 emulator-integration) under `functions/src/shared/`; `functions/vitest.emulator.config.ts`; `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-implementation-report-2026-07-25.md`.
- **Files modified:** `functions/vitest.config.ts`, `functions/package.json`, `package.json` (root — `emulators:validate` now runs real tests), `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md`, `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md`, `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 023), `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** `vitest.config.ts` exclude list, two new `package.json` scripts, one new Vitest config file — all test-infrastructure, no runtime dependency changes. **Application code touched:** `functions/src/shared/` only (new). **Live infrastructure touched:** none — all validation ran against the local Firebase Emulator Suite.
- **Risks:** none remaining unresolved — see the Implementation Report §7 for the full architectural-observation list (backoff-parameter disclosure, `userId`/`authUid` equivalence assumption, two minor Engineering Blueprint text inaccuracies discovered and disclosed, not corrected in the blueprint itself).

## 2026-07-25 — ENG-P1-002-CR1: Concurrency Safety Correction

- **Date:** 2026-07-25
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11), correction to `ENG-P1-002`.
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-002-CR1` — correct two concurrency-safety defects in the shared idempotency and outbox modules identified during `FAR-001` (Foundation Architecture Review Package, read-only evidence gathering).
- **Entry verification:** branch `chore/eng-p1-002-shared-foundation`, HEAD `cbcdb31` at task start, working tree clean; `ENG-P1-002` confirmed `Under Review` (PR #12 open, mergeable, CI green, unmerged).
- **Correction A (idempotency):** replaced the two-call, non-transactional `checkIdempotency` + `reserveIdempotencyKey` pattern with a single atomic `checkAndReserveIdempotencyKey` (one `db.runTransaction`); made duplicate-handling status-aware (`evaluateIdempotency` now distinguishes `"processing"` → `"in_progress"`, `"failed"` → retryable-as-`"new"`, `"completed"` → `"duplicate"`), so the dispatcher can no longer fabricate a success from an absent `responseSnapshot`.
- **Correction B (outbox):** the processor now atomically claims exclusive ownership (`claimOutboxEntry`, transaction-verified) before invoking a handler, with expired-claim recovery and rejection of a stale worker's later write (`applyOwnedTransition`), instead of the previous unprotected read-then-blind-update.
- **Mid-implementation decision gap, stopped and reported per this task's own instruction:** the originally-planned no-new-schema mechanism for Correction B (using Firestore's own `updateTime` document metadata as an ownership token) was empirically disproven by its own failing real-emulator tests — a Firestore write that sets a field to the value it already holds does not advance `updateTime`, which is exactly what a same-status reclaim does. Execution paused and reported the finding with options rather than force a broken guarantee or invent schema silently; the Founder authorized a minimal new field, `OutboxEntry.claimedAt?: Timestamp`, which was then implemented and verified. See the Correction Report §"Mid-Implementation Stop" for the full empirical evidence and reasoning.
- **Test-first (TDD) throughout, all 7 required concurrency scenarios proven against the real Firebase Emulator Suite, not mocks:** unit tests 92/92 (up from 87/87); emulator integration tests 23/23 (up from 14/14) across 3 files, including two-simultaneous-callers races for both idempotency and the dispatcher, expired-claim recovery, and stale-worker rejection for both the completion and retry transitions.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** exactly the 9 files required for the two corrections and their tests were touched; no unrelated module, no domain implementation, no Firestore Security Rules, no new deployed Cloud Function. `ENG-P1-002` remains `Under Review`, not `Complete`. PR #12 was not merged, per this task's explicit constraint.
- **Files modified:** `functions/src/shared/idempotency/idempotencyService.ts`, `.test.ts`, `.emulator.test.ts`; `functions/src/shared/commands/commandDispatcher.ts`, `.test.ts`, `.emulator.test.ts`; `functions/src/shared/outbox/outboxEntry.ts`, `outboxProcessor.ts`, `outboxProcessor.emulator.test.ts`.
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-CR1-concurrency-safety-correction-report-2026-07-25.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 024); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** `functions/src/shared/{idempotency,commands,outbox}` only. **Live infrastructure touched:** none — all validation ran against the local Firebase Emulator Suite.
- **Risks:** see the Correction Report §Risks — `CLAIM_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 minutes) and the "failed record is retryable" interpretation are both disclosed implementation choices, not TRD-specified; `reserveIdempotencyKey` was removed from the public surface (its one caller was updated, no other reference existed); `applyOwnedTransition` was exported to make the stale-worker guarantee directly testable; `OutboxEntry.claimedAt` is a genuine, Founder-authorized schema addition.
- **Rollback:** revert the correction commit(s) on `chore/eng-p1-002-shared-foundation` (branch not merged to `main`, so no merge-commit revert is needed) or reset to the pre-correction commit `cbcdb31`; no live Firestore collection exists yet for either affected collection.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-CR1-concurrency-safety-correction-report-2026-07-25.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-CR1-concurrency-safety-correction-report-2026-07-25.md).
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-implementation-report-2026-07-25.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-002-implementation-report-2026-07-25.md).

Targeted investigation — 70+ adversarial concurrent-pair stress-test calls against the exact `checkAndReserveIdempotencyKey` mechanism, run directly against a real Firestore Emulator instance outside the test suite, both sequential and truly-parallel — found **zero** reproducible defects, alongside directly-measured, highly variable multi-second Firestore-emulator transaction-retry latency. This points toward Firebase Emulator/CI-environment timing sensitivity rather than a deterministic code defect, but is disclosed as its own finding (Technical Review §7.1) and escalated to the Founder rather than unilaterally resolved, since it touches the exact guarantee `ENG-P1-002-CR1` exists to establish. No code was changed as a result (no reproducible defect was found to fix). - **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); decision preparation for the provider dependency blocking `ENG-P1-003`.
- **Task:** `DEC-PROV-005-PREP` — prepare an evidence-based Founder decision package for `DEC-PROV-005`. Decision preparation only: does not select or configure a provider, does not modify application code, adds no dependency, creates no provider account, exposes no secret, does not change `DEC-PROV-005` to `CONFIRMED`, does not begin `ENG-P1-003`.
- **Entry conditions verified before any research (all 8 passed, no stop condition):** PR #15 merged (`1b07b55be9fc92526e2067486ad6014972f4b980`); post-merge CI green on that exact commit after two disclosed reruns (2 emulator-suite assertion failures, zero code difference across all 3 runs — PR #15 was documentation-only, so this is a recurrence of the already-Founder-accepted emulator-timing residual risk from the `ENG-P1-002` Technical Review, not a new defect); `EIR-ENG-P1-002` confirmed `Administratively Closed`; `ENG-P1-002` confirmed `Complete`; `ENG-P1-003` confirmed `Blocked`; `DEC-PROV-005` confirmed `OPEN_PROVIDER` and confirmed as `ENG-P1-003`'s sole stated provider dependency; no existing document found that already resolves or fully evaluates `DEC-PROV-005`; Master Workflow, Programme, Decision Register, Engineering Blueprint, and TRD20 §20.22–20.36/TRD23 §23.23 reviewed before research began.
- **Research performed:** three qualified options evaluated — Firebase/Google Cloud native (Cloud Error Reporting + Logging + Monitoring + Trace), Sentry, and a bounded hybrid (Sentry for frontend only, native for everything else) — against 18 required evaluation criteria, using current official Sentry and Google Cloud documentation/pricing accessed 2026-07-26, with every claim labeled verified fact, reasoned inference, or provisional assumption. **Key finding (verified fact):** Cloud Error Reporting has no browser JavaScript SDK; its own documentation directs client apps to Firebase Crashlytics, which is Android/iOS-only — a confirmed gap against the decision's own explicit "frontend + server" question. Architecture-boundary analysis (§6 of the Evidence Pack) confirmed that infrastructure logs, business-domain operational logs, security monitoring, and audit records stay on Cloud Logging/Firestore regardless of which option is chosen — the actual open question is narrower than "pick a vendor."
- **Recommendation offered, not adopted:** Technical Lead recommends Option C (Sentry scoped to frontend only) for Founder discussion — explicitly distinguished from a decision, with what selecting it would and would not authorize stated in the Founder Brief.
- **Files created:** `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-005-error-monitoring-evidence-2026-07-26.md` (Evidence Pack); `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-005-founder-brief-2026-07-26.md` (Founder Decision Brief); `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-005-source-register-2026-07-26.md` (Source Register, 12 external + 13 internal sources); `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-005-proposed-updates-2026-07-26.md` (proposed-but-unapplied Decision Register text, labeled unapplied in three places); `docs/05-implementation/reports/DEC-PROV-005-PREP-decision-preparation-report-2026-07-26.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 029); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** none besides the two append-only logs above.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** the Decision Register, Engineering Implementation Programme, Coding-Agent Prompt Register, application code, dependencies, Firebase configuration, live infrastructure, EIR files, and Master Workflow sequencing are all untouched. `DEC-PROV-005` remains `OPEN_PROVIDER`. No option was marked approved. `ENG-P1-003` was not started.
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** none. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **Unresolved questions carried forward (not answered by this task):** whether error-monitoring stack traces could incidentally capture customer/payment-adjacent data (provider-independent); whether Sentry's EU-region hosting satisfies the existing `DEC-LEGAL-006` cross-border analysis (scoped to Google Cloud, not yet extended to a new third-party processor) if any Sentry option is later selected; whether Cloud Logging/Monitoring/Error Reporting fall under the same Google Cloud DPA/subprocessor list already evaluated for Firestore/Storage/Auth; direct re-verification of Cloud Logging/Monitoring pricing figures against a successful direct page fetch (this pass's Google Cloud pricing pages returned truncated content on every attempt; figures rest on corroborated search synthesis, disclosed as such).
- **Risks:** none from this task's own output — evidence-only.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this task's own commit — only new, additively-created files plus two append-only log entries; no existing file was edited.

## 2026-07-26 — ENG-P1-003-BP: Operational Observability Blueprint

- **Date:** 2026-07-26
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); design blueprint for `ENG-P1-003`'s monitoring-initialization deliverable.
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-003-BP` — produce the authoritative Engineering Blueprint for `ENG-P1-003`'s Operational Observability Layer. Design only: no implementation, no dependency, no Firebase configuration, no Sentry configuration, no provider account, no application-code change.
- **Entry conditions verified before writing (all 6 passed, no stop condition):** PR #17 merged (`494dca103b5970e332f64b9f9c9065ac893dc46a`); post-merge CI green on that exact commit (run `30200279870`, first attempt, no rerun needed); `DEC-PROV-005` confirmed `CONFIRMED` in the live Decision Register; `ENG-P1-003` confirmed `Ready`; no existing `ENG-P1-003` blueprint found by repository search; governing documents reviewed — Platform Constitution (found CP-013 "Observe Everything" as the governing principle), TRD20 §20.22–20.36, TRD22 §22.11 (monitoring initialization is a Phase 1 deliverable, not itself named in Phase 1 exit criteria), Decision Register, `ENG-P1-002`'s own blueprint (structural precedent) and implementation code (`logger.ts`, `operationalLog.ts`, `errorCategories.ts`, `platformError.ts`, `correlationId.ts`, `outboxEntry.ts`, `idempotencyRecord.ts`, read directly for exact field/status/category names), Master Workflow, Engineering Implementation Programme.
- **Design produced:** a 14-section blueprint — Observability Philosophy (grounded in CP-013), Architecture (3 embedded Markdown diagrams: primary data flow, where frontend diagnostics fits, correlation investigation flow), Event Taxonomy (13 categories, each mapped to destination/severity/retention/owner, every category traced to an existing `ErrorCategory`/`OutboxStatus`/`IdempotencyStatus` value — no new category invented), Logging Model (references, does not redefine, the closed `OperationalLog` shape and correlation-ID behavior `ENG-P1-002` already built), Frontend Diagnostics (behavior only — capture boundaries, offline handling, breadcrumbs, PII redaction, sampling, rate limiting, environment separation; explicitly no SDK code), Backend Observability, Correlation Strategy (the exact browser-to-outcome investigation chain the task brief specified), Incident Workflow (detection through postmortem, engineering workflow not organizational policy), Privacy (a closed six-item never-capture list), Environment Strategy (Development/Local/Testing/Staging/Production, stating only what differs for observability), Failure Modes (6 modes, unified by "observability is additive, never load-bearing"), Operational Metrics (9 recommended areas, no dashboard/alert created), Future Extensibility (6 named future capabilities — Payments, Rewards, Notifications, Reporting, Mobile Apps, Partner APIs — each shown to attach without redesign), and non-binding Implementation Sequencing.
- **Terminology mapping applied:** confirmed via `grep -rn "\btenant\b"` that "tenant" is not this platform's vocabulary (one unrelated hit, TRD8 Firebase Authentication context) — the blueprint maps the task brief's "tenant" language onto the platform's actual `businessId` field throughout.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no Sentry account, API key/DSN, dependency, Firebase configuration, Cloud Monitoring configuration, dashboard, alert, SDK implementation, or application-code change. `ENG-P1-003`'s tracker status remains `Ready` — this task does not itself move it, matching the precedent that `ENG-P1-002-PREP`'s own blueprint did not move `ENG-P1-002`'s status. `ENG-P1-003` implementation itself was not begun.
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/prompts/ENG-P1-003-engineering-blueprint-2026-07-26.md`; `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-BP-implementation-report-2026-07-26.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 031); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** none besides the two append-only logs above.
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** none. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none.
- **Risks:** none from this task's own output — a design document with no implementation surface. Two questions the blueprint itself carries forward as open, not silently resolved: the exact frontend redaction mechanism and the specific Cloud Monitoring alert thresholds — both correctly deferred to a future, separately-authorized implementation task.

## 2026-07-26 — ENG-P1-003-IMP-01: Observability Foundation

- **Date:** 2026-07-26
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); first bounded implementation stage of `ENG-P1-003`'s monitoring-initialization deliverable.
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-003-IMP-01` — implement the provider-independent observability foundation described in the approved Operational Observability Blueprint. Founder-authorized, explicitly bounded: no Sentry account creation, SDK installation, DSN creation, production provider integration, dashboards/alerts, deployment, or completion of the full `ENG-P1-003` work package.
- **Entry conditions verified before writing (all 12 passed, no stop condition):** PR #18 merged (`eea58dd013340e666dbe7f41c43b65806fbefbe4`); post-merge CI green on that exact commit (run `30202500418`, first attempt); `origin/main` current; worktree clean; `DEC-PROV-005` confirmed `CONFIRMED`; `ENG-P1-003` confirmed `Ready`; blueprint confirmed on `main`; no prior `ENG-P1-003` implementation found; no Sentry/competing dependency found; `ENG-P1-002` error/logging/correlation/metadata contracts reviewed directly from the live code; implementation scope confirmed consistent with the Programme and blueprint.
- **Implemented (TDD, Red-Green-Refactor throughout):** `apps/web/src/observability/` — `types.ts` (provider-neutral `DiagnosticsProvider` contract, no Sentry-specific concept, `captureException` returns `void` so it never exposes a provider event ID); `noopProvider.ts` (the only active provider this stage — no network call, no account, no SDK, never throws); `sanitize.ts` (recursive, depth- and cycle-bounded redaction of passwords/tokens/authorization headers/cookies/session secrets/API keys/payment-card data/personal-data key names, plus JWT/token-shaped value detection mirroring `logger.ts`'s own guard); `config.ts` (mirrors `env.ts`'s `loadEnv` pattern exactly — `enabled`/`provider`/`environment`/`release`, no DSN field, fails safely to disabled+noop on an unsupported provider identifier); `observabilityService.ts` (the sole application-facing entry point — applies sanitization and correlation context, never propagates a provider failure, no-ops entirely when disabled); `correlationContext.ts` (a minimal frontend correlation-ID carrier using the browser's native `crypto.randomUUID()`, mirroring the backend's exact "resolve, never regenerate" semantics — the backend's own generator is Node-only and unreachable from the browser, a disclosed judgment call grounded in the blueprint's own §4/§7 text); `errorBoundaryIntegration.ts` (a `createRenderErrorHandler` callback shaped for React's `componentDidCatch`, no UI component built); `index.ts` (composition-root barrel plus a `getObservability()` lazy singleton accessor mirroring `getAppEnv()` — the stable integration point a future stage imports, not wired into `main.tsx` since nothing consumes it yet).
- **Tests:** 53 new tests across 6 files, all 18 of this task's required test behaviors covered explicitly. Full `apps/web` suite: 84/84 passing (zero regression on the 31 pre-existing tests). `functions` unit tests: 92/92 passing, unaffected — `functions/` confirmed byte-identical to `origin/main` via `git diff --stat`.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no dependency added (`package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml`/`apps/web/package.json`/`functions/package.json` diffs all empty against `origin/main`); no `@sentry/*` import anywhere in `apps/web/src`; no DSN field on any type; no secret-pattern match; no `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`axios`/raw `http` call anywhere in the new module; `main.tsx`/`App.tsx` untouched; `functions/` untouched; Firestore Rules untouched; Firebase project linkage untouched; Phase 2 not begun; `BaseMetadata` untouched; no other decision resolved; no EIR edited.
- **Tracker update:** `ENG-P1-003` row moved `Ready → In Progress` in both the Engineering Implementation Programme and the Coding-Agent Prompt Register — `In Progress` is the exact term the Master Workflow §5 status vocabulary defines for "a detailed prompt has been issued and the coding agent is implementing," set by the Coding Agent on start, per that vocabulary's own "who may set it" column. **Not** marked `Complete`; no new status term was invented.
- **Files created:** `apps/web/src/observability/types.ts`, `noopProvider.ts` (+test), `sanitize.ts` (+test), `config.ts` (+test), `correlationContext.ts` (+test), `observabilityService.ts` (+test), `errorBoundaryIntegration.ts` (+test), `index.ts`; `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-implementation-report-2026-07-26.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 032); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** `apps/web/.env.example` (3 new optional, non-secret placeholder lines); `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/engineering-implementation-programme.md`; `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/coding-agent-prompt-register.md`.
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** 3 new optional `.env.example` placeholders, no secret, no DSN. **Application code touched:** `apps/web/src/observability/` only (new directory) — no existing application file modified. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **Deviations from blueprint:** none. One disclosed judgment call: the frontend correlation-context carrier generates its own UUID via the browser-native API rather than importing the backend's generator (a physical necessity, not a design deviation — see the Implementation Report §21).
- **Deferred, not begun this stage:** `ENG-P1-003`'s Security/Storage Rules deny-by-default scope; a real React `<ErrorBoundary>` component; a Sentry adapter; wiring the observability service into `main.tsx`/`App.tsx`; Sentry account creation, DSN issuance, dependency installation, production integration.
- **Risks:** none introduced — the no-op provider performs no I/O; the service never throws into calling code.
