## 2026-07-26 — ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-CR1: Provider-Boundary Privacy Correction

- **Date:** 2026-07-26
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); pre-merge correction to `ENG-P1-003-IMP-01`'s open PR #19.
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-CR1` — per Founder review decision "PR #19 — Changes required before merge." Correction only, applied directly to PR #19's existing branch (`feat/eng-p1-003-imp-01-observability-foundation`) — no new branch, no Sentry, no application wiring, no work-package expansion, no other stage begun, no merge.
- **Entry conditions verified before editing:** PR #19 confirmed open and unmerged; head confirmed as the Stage 1 implementation (`c91c44d`); the no-op provider confirmed as the only active `DiagnosticsProvider`; no provider SDK or external network integration found anywhere in `apps/web/src`; the blueprint's §9 privacy requirements and the governing task text re-read; the existing `sanitize.ts` and its test suite reviewed in full; the provider contract (`types.ts`) and service boundary (`observabilityService.ts`) reviewed together.
- **Finding confirmed:** the Stage 1 service sanitized only the structured `context` argument. The raw exception/thrown value passed to `captureException`, the raw string passed to `captureMessage`, the raw `breadcrumb.message`/`breadcrumb.category`, and the caller's `ObservabilityUserContext` object (no runtime allow-list enforcement) all crossed to the provider unsanitized. A separate configuration-semantics defect: the internal gate checked only `config.enabled`, not `provider.isEnabled()`, while `isEnabled()` checked both — the two could disagree.
- **Root cause:** sanitization discipline was applied to the one channel that looked most "structured" (`context`) and not extended to free-text channels or to runtime enforcement of the already-closed `ObservabilityUserContext` type.
- **Correction applied:** established the invariant "no uncontrolled application diagnostic value crosses into a provider without sanitization or an explicit approved allow-list rule" and applied it to every channel. Added `sanitizeText()` (new, in `sanitize.ts` — substring-scanning free-text redaction: JWT-shaped, authorization/bearer-prefixed, cookie-prefixed, payment-card-length digit runs, long token/API-key-shaped runs) and `sanitizeException()` (new file — a plain, provider-neutral representation: `name`/`message`/`stack` sanitized as text, custom own properties sanitized structurally via `Object.getOwnPropertyNames`, `cause` walked to a bounded depth of 3). `setUserContext` now strictly allow-lists `actorId`/`businessId`/`customerId` as validated strings into a fresh object, never forwarding the caller's object. `addBreadcrumb` sanitizes `message`/`category` in addition to the already-sanitized `data`. Unified `isActive()` as the single predicate `guarded()`/`flush()`/`isEnabled()` all consult, so "requested enabled" and "effectively active" can no longer disagree. Correlation-context lifecycle (`correlationContext.ts`) got an explicit disposition doc comment — no new runtime code — covering workflow begin/end, page-reload vs. route-navigation behavior, and the current dormant-risk status (nothing wired yet).
- **Contract change disclosed:** `DiagnosticsProvider.captureException`'s TypeScript signature is unchanged (`error: unknown`) but its documented meaning changed — the service now always passes a `SanitizedException`, never the raw thrown value; a future provider adapter must expect this shape.
- **Tests:** 31 new/changed tests, TDD throughout (RED confirmed before each GREEN) — 9 in `sanitize.test.ts`, 11 in the new `sanitizeException.test.ts`, 11 in `observabilityService.test.ts`. Full observability suite: 84/84 passing (was 53). Full `apps/web` suite: 115/115 passing, zero regression. `functions`: 92/92 passing, unaffected (`functions/` confirmed byte-identical to `origin/main` via `git diff --stat`). All 15 required CR1 test behaviors covered explicitly, including direct inspection of a capturing test provider's received arguments (confirmed a `SanitizedException` object reaches `captureException`, a redacted string reaches `captureMessage`, a redacted `message`/`category` reach `addBreadcrumb`, and extra fields are absent from what reaches `setUserContext`).
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no dependency added (`package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` diffs empty); no `@sentry` import anywhere; no DSN or secret-pattern match; no external network call in the module; `main.tsx`/`App.tsx` untouched; Firestore Rules untouched; no dashboard/alert created; `ENG-P1-003` remains `In Progress`, not marked `Complete`; PR #19 not merged.
- **Files created:** `apps/web/src/observability/sanitizeException.ts` (+ test); `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 033); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** `apps/web/src/observability/sanitize.ts` (+ test); `apps/web/src/observability/observabilityService.ts` (+ test); `apps/web/src/observability/types.ts`; `apps/web/src/observability/correlationContext.ts`; `apps/web/src/observability/index.ts`; `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-implementation-report-2026-07-26.md` (§27 correction section appended — §1–26 unchanged history).
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** `apps/web/src/observability/` only. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **Remaining limitations, disclosed:** `sanitizeText`'s long-token pattern can false-positive on non-secret long alphanumeric runs (e.g. build-hash filenames in a stack trace) — an accepted, deliberate tradeoff, not narrowed away since narrowing risks under-redacting a real secret of the same shape. Correlation-context automatic clearing (route-change/logout) remains unimplemented by design — documented as future application-wiring work, not built speculatively here.
- **Risks:** none introduced — the correction only narrows what reaches the still-no-op-only provider.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this correction's own commit on the existing PR branch — restores the pre-CR1 Stage 1 state exactly.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-implementation-report-2026-07-26.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-01-implementation-report-2026-07-26.md) §27.

- **Entry conditions verified before writing:** PR #19 merged (`c0e39545c861a9bc8336f5778508a191c24eb0dd`); post-merge CI verified green on that exact commit; fresh worktree from `origin/main`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean; worktree clean before any edit; `ENG-P1-003` confirmed `In Progress`; no Stage 2 file already existed (confirmed by repository search).
- **Pre-edit analysis and stop-and-ask resolutions:** direct inspection of the live application found it materially thinner than the task brief assumed — one placeholder route, no logout UI, no API/network abstraction layer (zero `httpsCallable`/`fetch` usage anywhere in `apps/web/src`, confirmed by grep) and no approved customer-facing error-fallback UX in `docs/01-product`. This triggered two of the task's own explicit stop-and-ask conditions; both were surfaced via `AskUserQuestion` and resolved by explicit Founder decision, not guessed. **Fallback UX:** a minimal, unbranded, temporary engineering placeholder only ("Something went wrong." + a single Reload action, no internal detail), explicitly not approved product UX, recorded as a carried-forward Product Experience governance gap. **Correlation scope:** frontend-only this stage — full lifecycle, isolation, cleanup, and concurrency safety, with frontend-to-backend propagation and backend-issued-ID adoption explicitly recorded as **Blocked — awaiting the governed frontend API/network abstraction layer**, not claimed as end-to-end-ready. A third gap (no logout mechanism) was found independently and resolved by hooking into Firebase Auth's existing `onAuthStateChanged` listener rather than inventing new UI.
- **Implemented (TDD, Red-Green-Refactor throughout):** `ErrorBoundary.tsx` (React error boundary at the application root, routes through the existing `createRenderErrorHandler`, renders the Founder-approved temporary fallback); `globalErrorHandlers.ts` (`window` `error`/`unhandledrejection` capture, module-level idempotency guard, registered directly in `main.tsx` to avoid `StrictMode` double-invocation); `correlationContext.ts` additions `beginWorkflow()`/`endWorkflow(id)` (compare-and-clear, so a superseded workflow's delayed cleanup cannot clobber a newer active workflow's ID); `authLifecycle.ts` (clears correlation and observability user context on a real Firebase sign-out event); `connectivityBreadcrumbs.ts` (`online`/`offline` breadcrumbs, same shape as `globalErrorHandlers.ts`); `RouteTracker.tsx` (route-change breadcrumbs via `react-router-dom`'s own `useLocation()`, renders nothing). All wired into `main.tsx` — the observability foundation's first real consumer.
- **Tests:** 29 new tests across 6 files (`ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` 7, `globalErrorHandlers.test.ts` 5, `correlationContext.test.ts` +5, `authLifecycle.test.ts` 4, `connectivityBreadcrumbs.test.ts` 5, `RouteTracker.test.tsx` 3). Full `apps/web` suite: 144/144 passing (was 115/115 at Stage 1/CR1 baseline), zero regression. `functions`: 92/92 passing, unaffected. One disclosed test-environment artifact: jsdom's default action for an unhandled `window` `"error"` event surfaced as an uncaught exception in the "stops reporting after unregister" test despite the assertion passing correctly — fixed with a test-only `preventDefault()` listener isolated to that one test, not a defect in `globalErrorHandlers.ts` itself.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no dependency added (`package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` diffs empty against `origin/main`); no `@sentry` import anywhere; no DSN or secret-pattern match; no external network call anywhere in `observability/`; Firestore Rules untouched; `functions/` untouched; no production deploy; no third-party account or credential of any kind; `ENG-P1-003` remains `In Progress`, not marked `Complete`; Stage 3/4 and Phase 2 not begun.
- **Files created:** `apps/web/src/observability/ErrorBoundary.tsx` (+ test), `globalErrorHandlers.ts` (+ test), `authLifecycle.ts` (+ test), `connectivityBreadcrumbs.ts` (+ test), `RouteTracker.tsx` (+ test); `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-02-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 034); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** `apps/web/src/main.tsx` (composition-root wiring — captures `auth` from `initializeFirebasePlatform`, calls `getObservability()`/`beginWorkflow()`/`registerGlobalErrorHandlers`/`registerConnectivityBreadcrumbs`/`registerAuthLifecycle`, wraps the render tree in `<ObservabilityErrorBoundary>`, adds `<RouteTracker>` inside `<BrowserRouter>`); `apps/web/src/observability/correlationContext.ts` (+ test, `beginWorkflow`/`endWorkflow`); `apps/web/src/observability/index.ts` (barrel exports for all five new modules).
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** `apps/web/src/observability/` and `apps/web/src/main.tsx` only. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **Correlation status, explicit (per Founder instruction not to overclaim):** frontend correlation lifecycle — implemented. Frontend diagnostic correlation — implemented. Correlation/identity clearing on sign-out — implemented. Frontend-to-backend propagation — not implemented, blocked. Backend-issued-ID adoption — not implemented, blocked. End-to-end correlation — not yet demonstrated.
- **Deferred, not begun this stage:** frontend-to-backend correlation propagation and backend-issued-ID adoption (blocked on the not-yet-built API/network layer); route-level/feature-level error boundaries beyond the single root boundary; a Sentry adapter and everything Stage 3 scope (account, DSN, dependency, production integration).
- **Risks:** none introduced — only already-reviewed, already-sanitizing Stage 1 code paths were connected to real browser/React/Firebase-Auth events; still no-op provider only, no new external surface.

## 2026-07-27 — ENG-P1-003-IMP-03: Frontend Diagnostics Provider Adapter

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); Stage 3 of `ENG-P1-003`'s monitoring-initialization deliverable, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P1-003-IMP-03" (Stage 3 only — Stage 4 not begun).
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-003-IMP-03` — implement the minimum approved Sentry-backed `DiagnosticsProvider` adapter behind the existing provider-neutral architecture. Founder-authorized, explicitly bounded: no Sentry account/organisation/project creation, no real DSN, no auth token, no source-map upload, no production activation, no self-merge.
- **Entry conditions verified before writing:** PR #20 approved by explicit Founder decision and merged (commit `56b828bdffdf5de5d98c46c1a2c4bb5f3ea757d0`); `origin/main` confirmed at that exact commit (zero divergence); post-merge CI verified green (run `30259380736`); fresh worktree, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean, worktree clean before any edit; all required Stage 2 pieces (error boundary, global/connectivity/route handlers, correlation lifecycle, auth-driven clearing, no-op provider integration) confirmed present **by direct inspection of the merged repository**, not the earlier report; full `apps/web` suite run against the merged commit first: 144/144 passing.
- **Pre-edit analysis:** the required 10-point review of the live `DiagnosticsProvider` contract, `observabilityService`, sanitization boundary, activation logic, no-op provider, composition root, env-var conventions, test/mocking patterns, build config, and provider-import restrictions was performed and stated in full before any code was written; no conflict was found between the live repository and the approved architecture.
- **SDK selected:** `@sentry/react@10.68.0` — the single minimum official package; React/Vite versions (React `^19.2.7`, Vite `^8.1.1`) confirmed compatible with its peer-dependency range first; its own dependencies (`@sentry/core`, `@sentry/browser`, `@sentry/conventions`) confirmed browser-only via `npm view` and a lockfile scan for backend/Node/CLI Sentry packages (empty). No separate `@sentry/browser` direct dependency needed. `functions/package.json` untouched.
- **Implemented (TDD, Red-Green-Refactor throughout):** `sentryProvider.ts` (the adapter — all 8 `DiagnosticsProvider` methods mapped, `Sentry.init()` called with `integrations: []` disabling every one of Sentry's own default automatic integrations by name — `breadcrumbsIntegration`, `globalHandlersIntegration` (would otherwise duplicate Stage 2's own capture), `httpContextIntegration`, `linkedErrorsIntegration`, `browserApiErrorsIntegration`, `browserSessionIntegration`, `dedupeIntegration` — confirmed by direct inspection of the installed SDK's own type declarations, not memory; no tracing, replay, profiling, or feedback integration added despite `@sentry/replay`/`@sentry/feedback` being unavoidable transitive package dependencies; `sendDefaultPii: false`; `beforeSend`/`beforeBreadcrumb` wired as defense-in-depth, not a sanitization substitute); `providerSelection.ts` (pure, unit-testable activation gate — enabled + provider=`"sentry"` + non-empty DSN, all three required, any missing falls back to the no-op provider, construction failure also falls back safely); `config.ts` extended minimally (`"sentry"` provider identifier, optional `dsn` field — a DSN is a public identifier by Sentry's own design, not a secret); `index.ts`'s `getObservability()` updated to call `selectProvider(config)`; a new ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule making the provider-import boundary (previously convention/doc-comment only) machine-enforced, verified to actually fire on a deliberate test violation.
- **Disclosed finding and fix (minimal, TDD-verified correction to already-merged code):** writing the correlation-ID end-to-end integration test with a real `crypto.randomUUID()`-shaped value (matching production) surfaced a genuine defect in Stage 1/CR1's `observabilityService.ts`: `sanitizedContext()` merged the correlation id into context *before* sanitizing, so `sanitize()`'s generic long-token pattern (`/^[A-Za-z0-9+/=_-]{20,}$/`, deliberately broad) silently redacted any UUID-shaped correlation id — directly contradicting that file's own documented "treated as an approved identifier... never redaction" guarantee. Undetected through Stage 1/CR1/Stage 2 because every prior test used a short placeholder id (e.g. `"corr-123"`, under the 20-character threshold) and the no-op provider discarded whatever it received regardless. RED reproduced with a real assertion failure (`expected '[REDACTED]' to be '31199c39-...'`) before any fix code was written; fixed by reordering `sanitizedContext()` to sanitize the caller's context first, then merge the trusted correlation id in afterward — a minimal, backward-compatible correction, not a redesign, with zero behavior change for any non-UUID-shaped id.
- **Tests:** 42 new tests across 8 files: `sentryProvider.test.ts` (18 — SDK init options, StrictMode-safe single-init, all 7 method mappings, resilience), `providerSelection.test.ts` (6 — every branch of the activation table), `sentryPrivacy.test.ts` (8 — end-to-end privacy through the *real* service + *real* adapter with only the SDK mocked: raw phone/email/loyalty-number/query-string-token/exception-property/breadcrumb/identity-field exclusion all confirmed absent from what the SDK mock actually receives), `sentryNetworkSafety.test.ts` (2 — explicit `fetch`-spy guard proving zero real network calls across the adapter's full method surface), `sentryIntegrationBoundaries.test.ts` (3 — correlation-ID mapping and clearing, Stage 2's `ObservabilityErrorBoundary` composing correctly with the Sentry adapter), plus `config.test.ts` (+3), `sanitize.test.ts` (+1, extending the closed key-substring list with `loyalty`/`qrcode`/`customername`), `observabilityService.test.ts` (+1, the defect-fix proof). Full `apps/web` suite: 186/186 passing (was 144/144), zero regression. `functions`: 92/92 passing, unaffected.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** exactly one new dependency (`@sentry/react`, its own transitive deps confirmed browser-only); no Sentry account/organisation/project created; no real DSN or credential anywhere (only a fake test-only DSN string inside fully-mocked test files); no Sentry configuration in `functions/` (byte-identical to `origin/main`); no application feature component imports `@sentry/react` (confirmed by scan and by the new, verified-firing ESLint rule); no source-map-upload tooling; Firestore Rules untouched; `ENG-P1-003` remains `In Progress`, not marked `Complete`; Stage 4/Phase 2 not begun; no self-merge.
- **Files created:** `apps/web/src/observability/sentryProvider.ts` (+ test), `providerSelection.ts` (+ test), `sentryPrivacy.test.ts`, `sentryNetworkSafety.test.ts`, `sentryIntegrationBoundaries.test.ts`; `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-03-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 035); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** `apps/web/package.json` (+ `pnpm-lock.yaml`), `apps/web/src/observability/config.ts` (+ test), `apps/web/src/observability/observabilityService.ts` (+ test — the disclosed fix), `apps/web/src/observability/sanitize.ts` (+ test), `apps/web/src/observability/index.ts`, `apps/web/.env.example` (new `VITE_OBSERVABILITY_DSN=` placeholder, blank), `eslint.config.js`.
- **Dependencies added:** one (`@sentry/react@^10.68.0`, direct). **Configuration changes:** one new blank placeholder env var. **Application code touched:** `apps/web/src/observability/` and `eslint.config.js` only. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **Deferred, not begun this stage:** every external Founder action (Sentry account/organisation/project creation, real DSN issuance, auth token, source-map upload, production activation); frontend-to-backend correlation propagation (still blocked, unchanged from Stage 2); Stage 4 (Operational Validation and Readiness).
- **Risks:** none introduced — the adapter is inert while disabled by default (the shipped state); the disclosed correlation-id fix reduces risk (a previously-false documented guarantee is now actually true) rather than introducing any.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this stage's own commit(s) on its dedicated branch — every change is additive or narrowly scoped; the one behavioral fix is independently correct and safe to keep even under a partial revert.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-03-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-03-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md).

## 2026-07-27 — ENG-P1-003-IMP-04: Operational Validation and Readiness

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); Stage 4 of `ENG-P1-003`'s monitoring-initialization deliverable, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P1-003-IMP-04" (validation-first, not a new implementation stage).
- **Task:** `ENG-P1-003-IMP-04` — validate the merged observability implementation and classify readiness across four independent states (Architecture/Integration/Staging/Production Ready). Founder-authorized, explicitly bounded: no broad new observability implementation, no external provider account, no real credential, no production activation, no third-party terms acceptance.
- **Entry conditions verified before writing:** PR #21 approved by explicit Founder decision and merged (commit `310313ea08779bb9c1502cbea31fa1182a5c821c`); `origin/main` confirmed at that exact commit; post-merge CI verified green (run `30277223179`); fresh worktree, clean install, clean tree before any edit; all 11 required Stage 3 pieces confirmed present **by direct inspection**, not the earlier report; full `apps/web` suite run against the merged commit first: 186/186 passing.
- **Pre-validation analysis:** the required 13-point review (full observability flow, provider selection, sanitization boundary, correlation lifecycle and its documented frontend-only limitation, error-boundary behaviour, global handlers, breadcrumbs, identity/sign-out clearing, adapter resilience, testing strategy, environment configuration, outstanding external Founder actions, blueprint/implementation gaps) was performed and stated in full before any code was written; no architecture conflict found.
- **Validation strategy and outcome:** cross-referenced every required checklist item (functional, privacy, correlation, resilience, security, regression) against actual test titles in the repository rather than assuming coverage. This surfaced two genuine gaps.
- **Defect 1 (fixed, TDD):** `sanitizeText()` had no pattern for a plain email address or general phone number embedded in free text (exception messages, `captureMessage` strings, breadcrumb text) — only a structured, key-named field (e.g. `{ email: "..." }`) was previously protected via `sanitize()`'s key-substring check. Verified with a standalone script first (confirmed `sanitizeText("...person@example.com...")` returned the email completely unredacted), then reproduced with two real failing tests, then fixed by extending the existing `TEXT_SCAN_PATTERNS` array with an email-address pattern and a broader phone-number-shaped pattern (7+ digits with separators), placed before the long-token pattern per that pattern's own "checked last" ordering rule. This is a minimal extension of an already-existing, already-designed mechanism — the same class of fix as Stage 3's own loyalty/QR/customer-name closed-list extension — not an architecture change.
- **Defect 2 (proof gap, not a code defect):** `RouteTracker.tsx`'s `React.StrictMode` double-invocation safety was previously only argued in a doc comment, never proven with a literal `<StrictMode>` render test. Added one; it passed on first execution, confirming the existing `useRef` guard was already correct — closed with evidence rather than reasoning alone.
- **Additional proof-only test:** "invalid or unsafe correlation-id values are handled safely" (a required correlation-validation proof point) had no direct test; added one confirming `setCorrelationId`/`resolveCorrelationId` never throw and correctly treat an empty string as "no id supplied." Passed immediately — no defect, evidence closed.
- **Tests:** 4 new tests total. Full `apps/web` suite: 190/190 passing (was 186/186), zero regression. `functions`: 92/92 passing, unaffected (untouched throughout).
- **Readiness classification (independent, none collapsed, none manufactured):**
  - **Architecture Ready — PASS.** Provider-neutral boundary preserved and now machine-enforced (ESLint rule); backend observability remains entirely separate; no-op provider remains the shipped default; provider selection deterministic; observability non-load-bearing (double-layered try/catch); every Sentry default automatic integration confirmed disabled by name against the installed SDK's own type declarations.
  - **Integration Ready — PASS.** All required event-capture paths (errors, rejections, breadcrumbs, identity, correlation, sign-out) proven end to end through the real service and real Sentry adapter (SDK mocked). Frontend-to-backend propagation remains not implemented — a documented, Founder-approved scope boundary from Stage 2, not a defect.
  - **Staging Ready — PASS WITH CONDITIONS.** The code path is fully implemented and would activate correctly given a real staging DSN; every unmet condition (Sentry organisation/project/terms/DSN, privacy review, access, retention, alerts, environment naming, source-map decision, execution of the manual validation plan) is external and Founder-owned, not a code defect — per the governing task's own explicit framing.
  - **Production Ready — NOT YET ASSESSABLE.** No staging evidence exists yet to ground a production assessment; not marked FAIL, since nothing has actually failed — this reflects absence of evidence, not negative evidence.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no dependency added; no Sentry account/organisation/project created; no real DSN or credential anywhere; no production activation; no source-map upload; no session replay/tracing/profiling; no backend file touched (`functions/` byte-identical to `origin/main`); Firestore Rules untouched; `ENG-P1-003` remains `In Progress`, not marked `Complete` — programme closure explicitly reserved for a separate, later Founder decision; no self-merge.
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-04-operational-readiness-report-2026-07-27.md` (the single authoritative readiness report, with Appendices A–G in place of separate overlapping documents: Staging Activation Checklist, Production Activation Checklist, Provider Onboarding Action List, Rollback Procedure, Privacy Verification Checklist, Manual Validation Plan, Known Limitations); `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-04-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md`; `docs/00-governance/documentation-changes-log.md` (Entry 036); `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** `apps/web/src/observability/sanitize.ts` (+ test — the free-text email/phone fix); `apps/web/src/observability/correlationContext.test.ts` (+1 proof test); `apps/web/src/observability/RouteTracker.test.tsx` (+1 `StrictMode` proof test).
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none. **Application code touched:** `apps/web/src/observability/sanitize.ts` only (production code); three test files. **Live infrastructure touched:** none. **Provider account created:** none. **Secret exposed:** none.
- **CI flakiness disclosure:** a fourth occurrence was recorded when this stage's own PR #22 was opened — a docs-only evidence-recording commit failed once on `idempotencyService.emulator.test.ts`'s "reports 'duplicate' with the stored response after completeIdempotencyKey" (identical test/signature to one seen during Stage 3's PR #21 review), then passed cleanly on rerun; `functions/` remained untouched throughout (empty diff confirmed before and after), and every deterministic CI step passed consistently. Combined with the three prior documented occurrences (Stage 2's PR #20 once, Stage 3's PR #21 twice, across three different specific tests within `functions/src/shared/{commands,idempotency}/*.emulator.test.ts`), all four remain accurately recorded and were not re-investigated or modified here, per this task's explicit instruction not to touch unrelated backend tests as part of `ENG-P1-003`. **Recommended, not implemented:** `ENG-CI-001 — Firebase Emulator CI Flakiness Investigation and Stabilisation`, as a separate follow-up task independent of any `ENG-P1-003` stage's merge readiness.
- **Deferred, not begun this stage:** every external Founder action listed in the readiness report's Appendices A–C (Sentry account/organisation/project creation, real DSN issuance for staging then separately production, terms acceptance, privacy/legal review, access/retention/alert decisions, source-map decision, production runbook); execution of the Manual Validation Plan against a real staging environment; `ENG-CI-001`; Stage 5 or any further `ENG-P1-003` work; administrative closure of `ENG-P1-003` itself.
- **Risks:** none introduced — both corrections this stage reduce risk (closing a real privacy gap; converting an assumption into proven behavior) rather than introducing any.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this stage's own commit(s) on its dedicated branch — both corrections are safe to keep independently even under a partial revert.
- **Report link:** [`docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-04-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md`](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-04-implementation-report-2026-07-27.md) and [the operational readiness report](../05-implementation/reports/ENG-P1-003-IMP-04-operational-readiness-report-2026-07-27.md).

## 2026-07-27 — ENG-P1-003-IMP-05: Engineering Closure and Handover

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); the administrative engineering-closure stage for `ENG-P1-003`, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P1-003-IMP-05: Engineering Closure and Handover". Audit and documentation-sync only — no new product capability, no architecture change, no feature work.
- **Task:** verify that all engineering work completed under `ENG-P1-003` has been merged, validated, and documented, and produce an auditable closure record and administrative recommendation. Closure decision itself explicitly reserved for the Founder.
- **Entry conditions verified before writing:** PR #22 approved and merged on explicit Founder authorization (one pre-merge review-comment correction applied first, below) — merge commit `243692006fefff03298528af02f6b50a6ae5c1bf`; `origin/main` fetched fresh, confirmed identical; post-merge CI verified green (one rerun of a pre-existing, unrelated `functions/` emulator flake — its fifth documented occurrence across this work package's history); fresh worktree, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean, `git status --short` clean; local `main` and `origin/main` both `2436920...`, zero divergence.
- **Pre-merge correction (PR #22, before this closure task's own work began):** an automated review (Codex, P2 severity) found the Stage 4 free-text phone-number redaction pattern measured total match length (digits plus separator characters combined) against its documented 7-digit minimum, rather than actual digit count — so a widely-spaced two-digit run such as `"1     2"` could satisfy the threshold and be falsely redacted despite containing only 2 real digits. Reproduced with a real failing test (`sanitizeText("values 1     2 differ")` returning `"values [REDACTED] differ"`), fixed by restructuring the pattern so the minimum is enforced per digit (`(?:[\s.\-()]*\d){6,}`, each repetition consuming exactly one digit) independent of separator whitespace, confirmed GREEN (191/191 full suite, typecheck/lint/format clean), pushed to PR #22's branch, CI re-verified green, then merged.
- **Engineering audit performed:** confirmed all five PRs merged with exact commits (`#18` Blueprint `eea58dd0`; `#19` `IMP-01`+`CR1` `c0e39545`; `#20` `IMP-02` `56b828bd`; `#21` `IMP-03` `310313ea`; `#22` `IMP-04`+pre-merge correction `24369200`); re-ran full regression against the final merged commit independent of any prior stage's own validation run (191/191 `apps/web`, 92/92 `functions`, clean typecheck/lint/build); confirmed zero architecture drift from the approved Blueprint (provider-neutral boundary intact and ESLint-enforced, `functions/` byte-identical to `origin/main` throughout, no backend Sentry, no production telemetry, observability remains non-load-bearing).
- **Requirements traceability finding, corrected before merge:** the original audit draft claimed `FR-SEC-006` (Firestore/Storage Rules deny-by-default) had zero implementation evidence — a P1 automated-review finding on this closure PR caught the error: `firestore.rules`/`storage.rules` exist at the repository root, `allow read, write: if false`, wired into `firebase.json`, established in Phase 0 (`ENG-P0-001`, commit `3a50710`), well before `ENG-P1-003` began. The original `git log` search used a glob pattern (`**/firestore.rules`) that failed to match the root-level files — a search-tooling bug, not a fact about the repository (`git log -- firestore.rules storage.rules` shows only the Phase 0 commit, confirming `ENG-P1-003` never touched them, but also confirming the deny-by-default posture predates and was never `ENG-P1-003`'s own work to build). What genuinely remains unbuilt (confirmed by `find . -iname "*rules*.test.*"`, zero matches) is formal, automated Rules testing and domain-specific (per-collection/per-role) authorization rules — the current posture is a deliberate, safe, blanket-deny placeholder, explicitly deferred by its own doc comments to future work. `FR-OPS-009`/`FR-OPS-010` are partially implemented (frontend capture complete; alert routing/dashboards remain an operational, not engineering, task per the readiness report's own Staging/Production conditions).
- **Documentation staleness found and corrected:** the Engineering Implementation Programme's `ENG-P1-003` narrative and Work-Packages table still described only Stage 1 (dated 2026-07-26), including the now-false sentence "a real error boundary, a Sentry adapter... remain not begun." The Coding-Agent Prompt Register's `ENG-P1-003` row likewise linked only the `IMP-01` report. Both corrected with append-only additions summarizing Stages 2–4 and this closure audit — no historical entry rewritten, consistent with this work package's own established discipline.
- **Administrative recommendation, corrected:** **`ENG-P1-003 COMPLETE`** — the observability scope (Stages 1–4) is complete, tested, documented, architecturally sound, and ready for Operational Enablement handover. `FR-SEC-006`'s literal requirement (deny-by-default) was already satisfied before this work package began, by unrelated Phase 0 work — not an unmet condition of this closure. The tracker `Status` field is left `In Progress` — the status change and closure decision itself remain with the Founder, not applied by this audit.
- **Follow-on work recommended, none implemented:** `OBS-OPS-001` (Frontend Diagnostics Operational Enablement — staging/production activation, provider onboarding, per the readiness report's own Appendices A–C); `ENG-SEC-001` (Firestore & Storage Security Rules Foundation — formal Rules testing and domain-specific authorization rules, building on the existing Phase 0 deny-by-default placeholder); `ENG-CI-001` (Firebase Emulator CI Stabilisation — now eight-plus documented occurrences across `ENG-P1-002`'s Technical Review and every `ENG-P1-003` stage PR, including this closure PR's own CI cycle).
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no external provider account created; no credential or DSN added; no diagnostics activated; no production deployment; no Firestore/Storage Rules modified; no Decision Register entry changed (`DEC-PROV-005` confirmed consistent, `CONFIRMED`, no drift — report only, no edit); no self-merge of the closure PR opened by this task; `ENG-P1-003` not administratively closed by this task.

- **Phase:** Phase 1 — Firebase and Shared Platform Foundation (TRD22 §22.11); administrative closure of `ENG-P1-003`, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P1-003 Administrative Closure", following Founder Decisions 1–3 on the `ENG-P1-003-IMP-05` closure audit.

Root cause: the original `git log --all -- "**/firestore.rules" "**/storage.rules"` search used a glob pathspec that does not match root-level files with no directory nesting, returning an empty result — a search-tooling bug, not a fact about the repository. ## 2026-07-29 — ENG-PROG-001: Capability Delivery Roadmap (CDR-001) Formalisation

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Phase:** Execution-layer documentation, following `ENG-P1-003`'s administrative closure, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-PROG-001: Capability Delivery Roadmap (CDR-001) Formalisation." Not a redesign of the Product Definition or the Engineering Implementation Programme; no new engineering work package created.
- **Task:** create a new execution-layer document bridging the Product Definition and the Engineering Implementation Programme, describing the sequence in which customer capabilities are delivered, re-grouping the 47 already-approved engineering work packages into 10 customer/platform-facing capabilities.
- **Required analysis performed before writing (disclosed in chat and in the accompanying implementation report):** reviewed the Product Foundation (`00-product-foundation.md`, including its distinct role-based "Capabilities" terminology, explicitly disambiguated from this document's capability-delivery-sequence usage); reviewed the Engineering Implementation Programme's full 17-phase, 47-work-package structure via the live Coding-Agent Prompt Register table; reviewed the approved customer journeys (`docs/07-product-design/moments-that-matter.md`'s 8 governed emotional-journey moments); reviewed the approved Stitch design references (`docs/07-product-design/stitch/exploration-v1/` and `exploration-v2/`, governed by `07-product-design/README.md`); cross-referenced every one of the 47 work packages' titles/requirement IDs directly from the live register before assigning it to a capability; independently re-verified the Decision Register status of `DEC-SEC-001`/`DEC-DATA-007`/`DEC-PROV-004`/`DEC-ID-003` before citing them.
- **Structure proposed and stated in chat before creation:** 10 capabilities (0–9, matching the Founder's own list), each grouping one or more existing Programme phases — Capability 0 = Phase 0, Capability 1 = Phase 1 (both already `Complete`), Capability 2 = Phase 2's customer slice, Capability 3 = Phase 2's business slice + Phase 3, Capability 4 = Phase 5 + Phase 6, Capability 5 = Phase 7, Capability 6 = Phase 8, Capability 7 = Phase 9 + 10 + 11, Capability 8 = Phase 12 + 14, Capability 9 = Phase 13. Phase 4 noted as a Capability 5 precondition without being re-tabled (avoiding double-counting against Phase 3's parallel sequencing); Phase 15/16 treated as milestone-level, not capability-level.

- **Deliverable:** `CDR-001-capability-delivery-roadmap.md`, 286 lines, 11 sections (the 10 required plus a disclosure section on this task's own constraints): Purpose (with explicit disambiguation from the Product Foundation's own "Capabilities" term); Relationship to Existing Documents (with an ASCII traceability diagram, Product Vision → Customer Journey → Capability → Engineering Work Package → Technical Implementation → Validation → Milestone); Guiding Principles (6); Capability Delivery Model (all 10 capabilities, each with objective/customer outcome/journey/work packages/dependencies/validation outcome/milestone contribution); Capability Timeline (ASCII progression diagram); Milestone Structure (Milestone A "First Complete Loyalty Cycle" fully specified against Moments That Matter's 8-step journey; three future milestones — Business Operational Readiness, Pilot Readiness, Production Readiness — named but explicitly left unspecified, since none is yet supported by approved documentation detailed enough to specify without inventing scope); Engineering Work Package Mapping table (only existing work packages, none invented); Stitch Usage Guidance (restating, not overriding, the existing Product Design section's own reference-not-specification framing); Definition of Capability Completion (extends, does not replace, the existing work-package-level Definition of Done); Future Engineering Prompt Standard (a 7-item mandatory preamble — Capability, Customer Outcome, Engineering Objective, Dependencies, UX References, Validation Criteria, Milestone Contribution — for future implementation prompts, layered on top of, not replacing, the existing Implementation Prompt Standard).
- **Validation performed:** Prettier clean; all 26 internal markdown links verified to resolve against the actual filesystem (files and directories) via a Python script, not assumed; §7's mapping table and §2's relationship table both confirmed structurally consistent (uniform column counts); every work-package title/requirement ID and every cited Decision Dependency status independently re-verified against the live source documents, not recalled from memory; confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat` that no file under `docs/01-product/` or `docs/05-implementation/change-tracking/` was modified by this task.
- **Assumptions disclosed:** the specific phase-to-capability groupings (e.g., "Capability 7 = Phases 9+10+11") are this task's own derived analysis, not a pre-existing Founder-approved mapping — stated as such, not presented as already-authoritative; `ENG-P2-004`'s shared placement across Capabilities 2 and 3 is likewise this task's own judgment call, disclosed in the implementation report.
- **Risks disclosed:** none to existing systems (purely additive documentation); a disclosed, unmitigated staleness risk — CDR-001 §7's status column will drift as work packages progress unless a future process keeps it synchronized with the Programme and Prompt Register, which this task does not itself establish.
- **Governance boundaries preserved:** no existing approved document modified (Product Foundation, Engineering Implementation Programme, PRDs, TRDs, Product Design section all unchanged); no new engineering work package invented; no requirement ID, decision ID, or work-package status altered; no duplicated authority — every fact CDR-001 states about a requirement, decision, or work package is a citation to where it's already governed, not a restatement asserted as new authority.

Founder review of the original document confirmed repository placement, capability mapping, authority boundaries, governance, and implementation quality were all correct; this amendment is the one identified improvement.
- **Change applied:** inserted a new `## 2. Capability Status Summary` section immediately after §1 Purpose — a single 3-column table (Capability | Name | Status) using only the four Founder-specified status values (`Planned`, `In Progress`, `Complete`, `Deferred`); no percentages, progress bars, dates, milestones, or engineering commentary, per the task's explicit constraints. Every section from the former §2 onward renumbered by one (through the former §11, now §12); all 34 of the document's own internal `§N` cross-references updated to match, via a full manual line-by-line audit against the pristine pre-amendment file rather than a blanket find/replace — necessary because many `§N` occurrences in the document are external references (Moments That Matter's own 1–8 numbering, TRD22 §22.xx) that share the same notation but must never shift. Three rounds of gaps were found and corrected during this audit, each traced to an overly broad exclusion filter in the automated first pass (lines mentioning "PRD" or "Product Design" elsewhere in the sentence were wrongly skipped even when they also contained a genuine self-reference) — each miss was caught by a subsequent full unfiltered re-scan before the work was considered complete.

Copying the example's literal value would have created an internal contradiction within the same document. Treated the example as illustrative formatting rather than literal required values; the new table records Capability 0 and 1 as `Complete`, Capabilities 2–9 as `Planned` — disclosed at the time in chat and in the implementation report, not silently corrected.

## 2026-07-29 — CDR-001 Pre-Merge Correction: 4 Review Findings

- **Date:** 2026-07-29 (same day, after Founder approval and merge authorization for PR #25, before the merge was actually executed)
- **Task:** as part of the standard pre-merge verification (no unresolved blocking review comments), 4 unresolved automated-review threads were found on PR #25's current head (2 P1, 2 P2, from Codex) that had not been present when CI was last checked. All four investigated against the live Engineering Implementation Programme before any change, per this task line's established discipline.
- **P1 — Capability 4 missing Phase 4 as a precondition:** the Programme states Phase 5 is `Blocked (depends on P4)`; the document had only noted `ENG-P4-001`/`ENG-P4-002` as a Capability 5 precondition, so Capability 4 could appear ready before a Reward Program existed to record a purchase against. Fixed: `ENG-P4-001`/`ENG-P4-002` moved into Capability 4's own work packages and §8 table row.
- **P1 — Capability 8/9 circular dependency:** Capability 8 grouped Phase 12 with Phase 14 (which the Programme states depends on `P0–P13 substantially complete`, i.e., including Phase 13 = Capability 9) while claiming Capability 8 depended only on Capability 1; Capability 9 was in turn stated to depend on Capability 8 — a genuine cycle. Fixed: Phase 14 regrouped into Capability 9 (with Phase 13); Capability 8's dependency corrected to Capability 7; the §6 Capability Timeline narrative (previously wrongly describing Capabilities 7/8/9 as parallel) corrected to state the true linear chain 7→8→9.
- **P2 — 5 of 47 work packages (`ENG-P15-xxx`/`ENG-P16-xxx`) unmapped despite §1's "47" framing:** §1 corrected to state 42 of 47 are capability-mapped, the remaining 5 explicitly identified as milestone-level; §7's Pilot Readiness and Production Readiness bullets now list exact work-package IDs, and Production Readiness's dependency corrected from "Capability 8" to "the Pilot Readiness milestone" (Phase 16 depends on Phase 15, not Phase 12 directly) — a further accuracy correction surfaced while fixing this finding.
- **P2 — `OBS-OPS-001` included in Capability 9's narrative but absent from its §8 table row:** since §10's completion definition only checks work packages "listed... in §8," Capability 9 could have been marked complete while `OBS-OPS-001` remained unimplemented. Fixed: added to the table row.
- **Self-caught fifth defect:** §10 item 1 still referenced "§7" instead of "§8" — a leftover miss from the earlier `ENG-PROG-001A` renumbering's own audit, found while re-reading the document to plan these fixes, not by the reviewer.
- **Validation performed:** full document re-read after all edits to check for inconsistencies introduced by the corrections themselves; Prettier clean; headers still sequential 1–12; all 28 links still resolve; a table-scoped script confirmed the §8 mapping table contains exactly 42 unique `ENG-Pn-xxx` IDs plus `OBS-OPS-001` (with `ENG-P2-004` legitimately shared across Capabilities 2 and 3, as already disclosed) — cross-checked against a manual capability-by-capability count.

## 2026-07-29 — ENG-P2-000: Capability 2 Readiness Review — Customer Identity

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Task:** a read-only planning and validation exercise, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P2-000: Capability 2 Readiness Review — Customer Identity," determining whether all prerequisites to begin Customer Identity implementation are complete, approved, and internally consistent. No code implemented; no governance document modified.
- **Areas reviewed:** Product readiness (PRD2, 770-line dedicated chapter); customer journey readiness (registration, sign-in, sign-out, profile access, account recovery); Moments That Matter; UX reference readiness (all Stitch v1/v2 concepts checked by title against Design Decisions Register cross-references); engineering architecture readiness (`apps/web/src` tree, Firebase Auth wiring, routing/form/state tooling); data readiness (TRD10 §10.6.1–10.6.2 `users`/`customerProfiles` schemas); security readiness (TRD12 §12.1–12.35); Founder decision dependency review (full live Decision Register search); CDR-001 capability dependency review; Engineering Implementation Programme work-package review (`ENG-P2-001`, `ENG-P2-004`).
- **Key finding — Programme's own classification independently re-verified, not assumed:** the Engineering Implementation Programme's Phase 2 profile already states Phase 2 is `Blocked — depends on Phase 1 and 4 D1 decisions (the largest concentration of D1 blockers of any phase)`. This review independently re-verified that classification against the live Decision Register, the `ENG-P1-002` Technical Review's `BaseMetadata` conflict, and the actual codebase, and confirms it: `DEC-SEC-001` (customer authentication approach), `DEC-PROV-004` (OTP delivery route), `DEC-ID-003` (permission inheritance), and `DEC-DATA-007` (loyalty number/QR generation algorithm — the capability's own core deliverable) all remain open, D1 priority. The `BaseMetadata`/TRD10 §10.5 field-shape conflict (disclosed at `ENG-P1-002`'s Technical Review, 2026-07-25) remains unresolved and is an explicit Phase 2 entry criterion in the Programme itself, independent of the decisions above.

Flagged for Founder/Engineering-Lead reconciliation; this review took no position and made no edit to either document, per the task's explicit constraint not to redesign either.
- **UX reference finding:** every approved Stitch concept (v1 and v2) was checked by title against the Design Decisions Register's own concept-matching table (§DEC-UX-001) — zero cover Registration, Sign In, or Profile; all are post-authentication screens (customer home, purchase verification, progress, redemption, business dashboard, notifications, navigation). Consistent with Moments That Matter's own disclosed gap for Registration, not new information.
- **Positive findings:** Capability 1 (Platform Foundation) confirmed `Complete`; PRD2, TRD10, and TRD12 are unusually mature and internally consistent (e.g., `UserDocument.status`'s 6-value enum matches PRD2 §7's 6 account statuses exactly — verified, not assumed); `ENG-P1-001`'s Firebase Auth SDK bootstrap and `ENG-P1-003`'s auth-lifecycle correlation-clearing hook are already implemented, tested, and wired; `ENG-P2-001`/`ENG-P2-004` remain appropriately scoped in the Programme with no redesign needed.
- **Recommendation: Not Ready** — a direct restatement of the Programme's own existing classification, independently re-verified rather than accepted at face value. Full readiness matrix and evidence in the report.
- **Validation performed:** every requirement ID, decision ID, decision status, and code reference cited was checked against its live source before inclusion (`grep`/direct reads), not recalled from earlier session context or assumed.

## 2026-07-29 — ENG-P2-000A: Phase 2 Governance Reconciliation

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Task:** a focused, read-only governance reconciliation for the issues `ENG-P2-000` identified, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P2-000A: Phase 2 Governance Reconciliation." No code implemented, no governance document modified, no new Founder decision created.
- **Method:** every decision and document `ENG-P2-000` cited was re-read **in full**, not from the truncated 5-line excerpts the original review used — this surfaced several fields (`Founder decision required`, `Decision owner`, `Dependencies`) the original review had not captured.
- **Key finding — Founder decisions are not a single undifferentiated blocker:** `DEC-DATA-007` requires **no Founder involvement at all** ("Founder decision required: No," Engineering-Lead-owned, no dependencies) and `DEC-ID-003` requires **only** a self-contained Founder judgment call (no external or other-decision dependency, already on "founder agenda Batch C" per its own Notes field) — both immediately actionable. `DEC-SEC-001` and `DEC-PROV-004`, by contrast, are **mutually dependent on each other** (each decision's own `Dependencies` field names the other) and both ultimately require `EXT-TECH-001` — a real-world, non-repository technical proof of Burundi OTP delivery feasibility that no governance or documentation work can substitute for.
- **Key finding — the `BaseMetadata` conflict is not a Founder-blocking item.** The Version 1 Engineering Blueprint's own §0 states, verbatim: "If this Blueprint and a TRD chapter ever appear to disagree, the TRD chapter governs." TRD10 §10.5 is therefore already authoritative over Blueprint §3.3 by the Blueprint's own pre-existing rule — no new Founder decision is needed. A genuine four-point field-shape conflict was confirmed by direct comparison (field naming `version`/`schemaVersion`; audit-field nullability; delete/archive semantics `deletedAt`/`archivedAt`; scoped-field sets `languageCode` vs. `currencyCode`/`timezone`). Additionally found: the already-shipped `functions/src/shared/metadata/baseMetadata.ts` (built under `ENG-P1-002`, `Complete`) implements the **Blueprint's** shape, not TRD10's — the correction is retroactive, not merely forward-looking for Phase 2.
- **Key finding — `DEC-LEGAL-005` vs. the Programme is a genuine Repository Error, not stale documentation.** The Decision Register's own explicit fields for `DEC-LEGAL-005` read `Priority: D2`, `Required by: Phase 2`, `Blocks: registration policy text`. The Engineering Implementation Programme's Phase 2 profile instead states it is "D3/pilot-tier, not a Phase 2 blocker." `D3` is a real, formally-defined priority tier used correctly elsewhere in the Register (`DEC-PROV-006`, `DEC-PROV-007`, `DEC-LEGAL-002`) — it is simply the wrong tier for this specific decision. `git log` confirms the Register's `DEC-LEGAL-005` entry has never been altered, ruling out a stale-tracking explanation. This changes Phase 2 readiness narrowly: it blocks registration policy text specifically (per the Register's own words), not the technical build — and the underlying Founder+legal-adviser obligation on `DEC-LEGAL-005` itself was already real and unaffected by this correction.
- **Recommendation: Still Not Ready** — the same overall conclusion as `ENG-P2-000`, now backed by a materially more precise account distinguishing which blockers are immediately actionable (`DEC-DATA-007`, `DEC-ID-003`, the `BaseMetadata` correction) from the one genuine hard blocker this reconciliation could not resolve or soften (`DEC-SEC-001`/`DEC-PROV-004`, gated on external technical proof).

## 2026-07-29 — ENG-P2-000B: Dependency Resolution Analysis

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Task:** a focused, read-only analysis of the `DEC-ID-003`/`DEC-SEC-001`/`DEC-PROV-004`/`DEC-DATA-007`/`DEC-LEGAL-005`/`EXT-TECH-001` dependency chain, per Founder task "TASK — ENG-P2-000B: Dependency Resolution Analysis." No code implemented, no governance document modified, no Founder decision resolved.
- **Key finding — `EXT-TECH-001` is the sole structural blocker.** It is a real-world technical proof (Firebase phone-OTP delivery to Burundi numbers), `Status: PENDING`, owner Engineering Lead, with zero dependencies of its own. `DEC-PROV-004` and `DEC-SEC-001` both depend on it and cannot close without it; `DEC-DATA-007` and `DEC-ID-003` have no dependency on it at all and are independently actionable today.
- **Key finding — the `DEC-SEC-001`↔`DEC-PROV-004` mutual dependency is not a genuine circular deadlock.** Read against each decision's own question text, a valid one-directional resolution order exists (`EXT-TECH-001` → `DEC-PROV-004` → `DEC-SEC-001`) — `DEC-PROV-004`'s delivery-route question is answerable directly from `EXT-TECH-001` evidence, and `DEC-SEC-001`'s fallback-definition component benefits from knowing `DEC-PROV-004`'s answer first. Classified as a repository modelling issue (the Register's `Dependencies` field cannot express ordered co-resolution against a shared external gate), not a true circularity.
- **Key finding — the Programme's flat "4 D1 decisions blocked" framing is not optimal sequencing.** Only `DEC-SEC-001`/`DEC-PROV-004` are externally gated; `DEC-DATA-007` (no Founder involvement) and `DEC-ID-003` (self-contained Founder decision, already on "founder agenda Batch C") could close in parallel with `EXT-TECH-001` evidence-gathering, requiring no new engineering work package and no Programme redesign.
- **Key finding — `DEC-LEGAL-005` sits outside this dependency chain entirely.** Its only dependency is `EXT-LEG-004` (a separate, unrelated external legal review), and per its own Register fields it blocks "registration policy text," not any named Capability 2 work package.
- **Resolution order produced:** a 7-step table (§5 of the report) distinguishing the one genuinely sequential chain (`EXT-TECH-001` → `DEC-PROV-004` → `DEC-SEC-001`) from the fully parallel, independently actionable items (`DEC-DATA-007`, `DEC-ID-003`), plus the out-of-chain `DEC-LEGAL-005` documentation correction and a final Programme-table synchronization step.
- **Validation performed:** every dependency edge in the graph was read directly from its source's own `Dependencies`/`Blocks` field (Decision Register or External Dependencies Register), not inferred; cross-checked `EXT-TECH-001`'s own `Blocks` field against both dependent decisions' `Dependencies` fields for mutual consistency (confirmed consistent); the one inference made (`DEC-PROV-004`'s Founder-involvement level, drawn from the absence of a field rather than an explicit statement) is disclosed as an assumption, not presented as a directly confirmed fact.
- **Files created:** `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P2-000B-dependency-resolution-analysis-2026-07-29.md`; `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** none.
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none.
- **Risks:** none introduced — read-only analysis; no code, decision, or governance document changed.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this task's own commit — a single new report file plus one changes-log append.

## 2026-07-29 — ENG-P2-000B: Pre-Merge Correction (PR #27 Review Findings)

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Task:** correction of three automated-review findings (P2 severity) on PR #27, raised against `docs/05-implementation/reports/ENG-P2-000B-dependency-resolution-analysis-2026-07-29.md` before Founder-authorized merge.
- **Finding 1 — the DEC-PROV-004/DEC-SEC-001 literal circular edge was reasoned around rather than resolved.** The report's original §4/§5 concluded from the decisions' own question text that a valid resolution order exists (`EXT-TECH-001` → `DEC-PROV-004` → `DEC-SEC-001`) and proceeded to sequence Steps 2/3 accordingly. The Decision Register's own governance rule (§1: agents "may never... infer a decision from an example") makes the literal `Dependencies` field authoritative — `DEC-PROV-004`'s field still names `DEC-SEC-001`, which remains open until the step after it. §5 now inserts a required governance-prerequisite step (formally waiving/correcting the edge, or closing both decisions as one combined action) before the sequential closure can proceed; §4, §8, §9, and §10 were updated to disclose this as a required governance action rather than an inference this report could authorize on its own.
- **Finding 2 — `DEC-PROV-004` was misclassified as requiring no Founder involvement.** The Decision Register's §1 governance rule states register-wide: "Engineering, provider and legal items are approved by their named owner and, where they affect product behavior, countersigned by the founder." `DEC-PROV-004` (customer OTP delivery route) directly affects authentication behavior. Corrected §1, §5 (Step 3), §6, §9, and §10 to require Founder countersign rather than treating the entry's lack of an explicit per-decision field as "no Founder involvement."
- **Finding 3 — `ENG-P2-001`'s blocking set was understated as "exactly two" open items.** The original §7 excluded `DEC-DATA-007` from the count because it has no dependency of its own, but the Programme's own table lists it as a current Decision Dependency, Precondition, and open Blocking Reason for `ENG-P2-001` — having zero dependencies makes it independently *actionable*, not *not currently blocking*. Corrected §7 (and cross-references in §5 Step 8, §8) to state `ENG-P2-001` has three open items against it, only two of which sit on the externally-gated critical path.
- **Validation performed:** all three findings verified directly against the live Decision Register (`§1` governance-rule text; `DEC-PROV-004`'s literal `Dependencies` field) and the Engineering Implementation Programme's Phase 2 Work-Packages table before any correction was applied; step renumbering (1→8, was 1→7) checked line-by-line for consistent cross-references across §5, §8, §9, §10; `npx prettier --check` on the modified file; `git diff --stat` confirmed only the one report file changed.

## 2026-07-29 — RES-001: EXT-TECH-001 Engineering Evidence Package

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Task:** gather and document engineering evidence to resolve `EXT-TECH-001` (Firebase phone-OTP delivery to Burundi numbers), per the Founder-authorized Resolution Sprint task "TASK — RES-001: EXT-TECH-001 Evidence Package." Evidence-gathering only — no governance decision made, no code implemented, no application code modified.
- **Key finding — product identification correction.** Classic Firebase Authentication phone sign-in (SMS OTP, the product `TRD12` §12.4.1/`OTD-004` actually specify) is a different product from Firebase Phone Number Verification (PNV), a newer carrier-network silent-verification product whose own pricing documentation lists only 8 countries. An initial research pass surfaced PNV's narrow list first; conflating it with classic phone sign-in would have produced a false "Burundi is unsupported" conclusion. The distinction is documented explicitly in the evidence package to prevent this error recurring.
- **Key finding — Burundi telecom infrastructure is technically capable of SMS delivery.** Three carriers (Lumitel, Econet Leo, Onatel) with near-universal 2G coverage (~97%), the relevant coverage figure for SMS since it rides on 2G/GSM signaling rather than data connectivity.
- **Key finding — Firebase has no first-party country restriction list for classic phone sign-in.** Delivery is instead gated by a per-project SMS Region Policy (default: no regions allowed) that must be explicitly configured — an operational prerequisite for `RES-002`/`RES-003`, not a support gap.
- **Key finding — the central evidence gap cannot be closed by documentation research.** No first-party Firebase/Google source confirms or excludes Burundi delivery specifically. A direct, real-SMS delivery test against all three carriers is identified as the single decisive remaining action, recommended as a bounded precursor step rather than a blocker to starting `RES-002`.
- **Engineering recommendation:** evidence is sufficient to draft `DEC-PROV-004`'s options and criteria, not sufficient to close it without the delivery test above.
- **Validation performed:** all repository citations verified against live file content; all external claims verified via live web research (`WebSearch`/`WebFetch`) against Firebase's own documentation and third-party telecom/SMS-aggregator sources, cited inline; repository-wide search confirmed no phone-auth-specific code exists in `functions/src` (no implementation has begun); `npx prettier --check` clean.
- **Files created:** `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/EXT-TECH-001-engineering-evidence-package-2026-07-29.md`; `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files modified:** none.
- **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none — the Blaze billing account, SMS Region Policy, reCAPTCHA, and test-phone-number setup identified are documented prerequisites for future tasks, not configured by this one.
- **Risks:** none introduced — evidence-gathering only; no code, decision, or governance document changed.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this task's own commit — a single new evidence-package document plus one changes-log append.
- **Report link:** [`docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/EXT-TECH-001-engineering-evidence-package-2026-07-29.md`](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/EXT-TECH-001-engineering-evidence-package-2026-07-29.md).

## 2026-07-30 — RES-002: DEC-PROV-004 Decision Package

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Task:** prepare the engineering decision package for `DEC-PROV-004`, using `RES-001`'s Engineering Evidence Package as primary technical evidence, per the Founder-authorized Resolution Sprint task "TASK — RES-002: DEC-PROV-004 Decision Package." Prepares the decision only — does not record, approve, or close `DEC-PROV-004`; no Decision Register field was changed.
- **Options evaluated:** Option A (Firebase-native OTP, already integrated into this repository's Auth client) and Option B (external SMS route via Africa's Talking, requiring a new custom OTP service). A third option (Twilio) was investigated but not presented as a full candidate since its Burundi support is unconfirmed by public documentation — presenting it as equally supported would have overstated `RES-001`'s evidence.
- **Preferred recommendation:** Option A, conditional on a real-SMS delivery test against all three Burundi carriers before final Founder countersign is treated as unconditional. Rationale: both options share the same unresolved delivery-reliability uncertainty (neither is empirically confirmed for Burundi), but Option B adds a materially larger, avoidable implementation-risk layer (custom OTP lifecycle service, second vendor, custom abuse controls) without evidence that it resolves the shared uncertainty.
- **Decision readiness:** Ready with Conditions — sufficient evidence exists for Founder review and provisional approval of the preferred direction; final, unconditional closure requires (1) the real-SMS delivery test `RES-001` identified, and (2) separate resolution of the governance-prerequisite edge between `DEC-PROV-004` and `DEC-SEC-001`'s `Dependencies` fields (disclosed here, not performed — that action belongs to a future, explicitly-scoped step per the Resolution Plan's own `RES-002` framing).
- **Governance risk disclosed, not resolved:** the Resolution Plan's own `RES-002` scope requires a formal governance action addressing the literal `DEC-PROV-004`↔`DEC-SEC-001` dependency edge before the decision can actually close under the Decision Register's own rules. This task brief's scope (prepare the decision package only) did not ask this package to perform that action, and it remains an explicitly flagged open item.
- **Validation performed:** every recommendation traced directly to `RES-001`'s Engineering Evidence Package and its own Source Register; live Decision Register entries for `DEC-PROV-004`/`DEC-SEC-001` re-confirmed unchanged since `RES-001`; no new external research performed (out of this task's own scope); `npx prettier --check` clean; `git status --short` confirmed only the intended two files changed.
- **Files created:** `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-004-decision-package-2026-07-30.md`; `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).

## 2026-07-30 — RES-002A: Pre-Merge Correction (PR #31 Review Findings)

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Task:** correction of three automated-review findings (P2 severity) on PR #31, raised against `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-PROV-004-decision-package-2026-07-30.md` after the `RES-002A` restructure, before Founder-authorized merge.
- **Finding 1 — §5 Option A's "Benefits"/"Engineering implications" text overstated Option A's readiness.** The original text claimed "zero additional integration work beyond configuration" and that the Auth SDK was "already present," implying application-level phone sign-in was effectively done. Confirmed false: a repository-wide search (`grep -rn "RecaptchaVerifier\|signInWithPhoneNumber\|ConfirmationResult" apps/web/src functions/src`) returned zero matches, and a full read of `apps/web/src/infrastructure/firebase/auth.ts` confirmed it contains only generic Auth-client initialization (`getAuth`/`connectAuthEmulator`), no phone-auth-specific code. Corrected §5 to disclose that the real sign-in flow (`RecaptchaVerifier` setup, `signInWithPhoneNumber` call, confirmation-code UI/handling) is not yet built, while preserving the correct conclusion that Option A's scope is still materially smaller than Option B's fully custom OTP service. Propagated the same correction to §6's Comparative Evaluation table ("Engineering complexity" cell, Low/configuration-only → Moderate/SDK-provided-backend-but-flow-not-built) and to §7's Engineering Recommendation reasoning (removed "zero additional engineering scope," replaced with the corrected scope description) — the recommendation itself (Option A) is unchanged.
- **Finding 2 — cost was evaluated nowhere in the package despite `RES-001` explicitly deferring it here.** Confirmed via re-reading `EXT-TECH-001-engineering-evidence-package-2026-07-29.md` §9, which states cost comparison between the two routes "is a `DEC-PROV-004` question, not resolved here" — a deferral this package never actually addressed (no Africa's Talking pricing, no cost row in §6). Added a new "Cost" row to §6's Comparative Evaluation table (both options disclosed as unmeasured/unknown for Burundi specifically), and added explicit disclosure that cost remains an unresolved, open item (not a basis for the recommendation) to §7 (recommendation basis clarified), §11 (new "Cost risk" bullet), and §12 (Decision Readiness, now naming three distinct open follow-on items instead of two).
- **Finding 3 — §11's "Operational risk" bullet self-contradicted §5.** §11 originally stated the SMS Region Policy misconfiguration risk applies "regardless of which option is chosen," directly contradicting §5's own statement that Option B "bypasses Firebase's own phone-auth mechanism entirely" (Option B does not use the SMS Region Policy at all). Corrected §11 to scope this risk to Option A specifically, and to note Option B would instead carry its own distinct third-party-provider (Africa's Talking) configuration/coverage risk, not evaluated in the same depth since Option A is the recommended option.
- **Validation performed:** all three findings verified directly against live repository code (`grep`, direct file read) and live document cross-references (`RES-001` §9, this package's own §5/§6/§7/§11/§12) before any correction was applied; `npx prettier --check` on the modified file; `git status --short` confirmed only the one decision-package file changed (plus this changes-log entry).

## 2026-07-30 — RES-003: Pre-Merge Correction (PR #33 Review Finding)

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Task:** correction of one automated-review finding (P1 severity) on PR #33, raised against `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-SEC-001-decision-package-2026-07-30.md` before Founder-authorized merge.
- **Finding (P1) — Option A's recommendation rested on a technically incorrect account-linking premise.** The original package described email-link sign-in as resolving "to the same canonical phone-number identity via account linking," and rated this "low identity-linking complexity" versus Option B's (Google Sign-In) "unsolved design item." Confirmed via direct verification: Firebase account linking (`linkWithCredential`) attaches a new credential to an **already-authenticated** session; in `DEC-SEC-001`'s actual trigger scenario — a customer's *first* phone-OTP attempt fails, so no phone-authenticated session was ever established — there is nothing to link to. Completing email-link sign-in in that moment creates a new, unlinked Firebase UID, the exact duplicate-identity outcome TRD12 §12.5 ("All linked providers shall resolve to one Firebase Authentication user") and AIR-001 ("One Firebase Authentication UID shall map to one active platform user") prohibit — confirmed by direct re-read of TRD12 §12.5/§12.6 before correcting. Option B carries the identical gap, not a worse one; the original asymmetric framing was itself the error.
- **Correction applied:** §5 (both Option A and Option B limitations rewritten to disclose the shared, undesigned identity-resolution problem); §6 (Comparative Evaluation table's identity-complexity row and "Engineering complexity" row relabeled for accuracy; "Reading the table" corrected); §7 (Engineering Recommendation's supporting rationale corrected — Option A preferred on non-identity-resolution grounds only); §8 (Engineering-recommendation bullet updated; a pre-existing, unrelated cross-reference error also fixed — "Operational conditions (§9 below...)" corrected to "§10 below," since §9 is Implementation Prerequisites and §10 is Operational Conditions); §9 (a new, first-listed Implementation Prerequisite naming the identity-resolution flow as a genuine, undesigned requirement); §11 (Risk Assessment's duplicate-identity risk elevated from "Option-B-only, future" framing to the primary, present-tense risk against implementing the recommended Option A itself); §12 (Decision Readiness now names the identity-resolution gap as an explicit condition rather than a minor caveat); §13 (Founder Briefing's options/conditions/risks sections updated to match); §1 (Executive Summary discloses the correction and points to the affected sections).
- **What did not change:** the overall recommendation (Option A, per the non-identity-resolution criteria) and the Ready-with-Conditions readiness determination are unchanged — the correction adds a named, load-bearing condition rather than reversing the recommendation, the same pattern `DEC-PROV-004` itself used for its own Identity Linking design gap.
- **Validation performed:** the finding verified by direct technical reasoning about Firebase's `linkWithCredential` API contract cross-checked against the package's own stated trigger scenario; TRD12 §12.5 ("Account Linking") and §12.6 ("Account Identity Rules," AIR-001–AIR-006) re-read directly to confirm the reviewer's citation before accepting it; `npx prettier --check` on the modified file; `git status --short` confirmed only the one decision-package file changed.
- **Files modified:** `docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-SEC-001-decision-package-2026-07-30.md`; `docs/changes/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES.md` (this entry).
- **Files created:** none. **Dependencies added:** none. **Configuration changes:** none.
- **Risks:** none introduced — the correction narrows the package's own claims to match verified Firebase API behavior and TRD12's identity rules; no code, Decision Register field, or architecture changed; `DEC-SEC-001` remains unrecorded.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` of this correction's own commit.
- **Report link:** [`docs/00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-SEC-001-decision-package-2026-07-30.md`](../00-governance/decisions/evidence/DEC-SEC-001-decision-package-2026-07-30.md).
